# Integer multiplication below n log n

Coordinate `EN-114-000-0007-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M1-C-AL-H-0237` | `PM-109.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Integer-multiplication-below-n-log-n-September-23-2026` | date 2026-09-23 | derived from the openai/math preprint Integer-multiplication-below-n-log-n-September-23-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We give a deterministic algorithm that multiplies two <i>n</i>-bit integers in $`O(n(\\lg n)^{1-\\kappa})`$ worst-case time, with $`\\kappa=2^{-182}`$, on one fixed finite-alphabet Turing machine with a fixed finite number of one-dimensional tapes. The algorithm is exact for every input length and disproves the $`n\\log n`$ optimality conjecture of Schönhage and Strassen in this model.",
  "canonical_anchor": "pm-s7",
  "claim_status": "CORPUS_CLAIMED",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give a deterministic algorithm that multiplies two <i>n</i>-bit integers in $`O(n(\lg n)^{1-\kappa})`$ worst-case time, with $`\kappa=2^{-182}`$, on one fixed finite-alphabet Turing machine with a fixed finite number of one-dimensional tapes. The algorithm is exact for every input length and disproves the $`n\log n`$ optimality conjecture of Schönhage and Strassen in this model.

- **claim-exact-multiplication** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is one deterministic Turing machine $A$, with a fixed finite alphabet and a fixed finite number of one-dimensional tapes, that computes this exact product for every $n\ge1$ and every pair of $n$-bit inputs. Its worst-case running time satisfies $$T_A(n)=O\!\left(n(\lg n)^{1-\kappa}\right), \qquad \kappa=2^{-182}.$$
  - Explanation: A single deterministic machine computes the exact padded binary product at every input length, with a strict power saving in the logarithmic factor. The alphabet and tape count remain fixed.
- **claim-exact-division-square-root** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-cor-exact-arithmetic)): There are deterministic machines with fixed finite alphabets and fixed finite numbers of one-dimensional tapes performing the following tasks in $O(n(\lg n)^{1-\kappa})$ worst-case time, for every $n\ge1$. For $x,y\in\{0,1\}^n$, put $a=\operatorname{val}(x)$ and $b=\operatorname{val}(y)$. If $b>0$, the division machine takes $x\#y$ and returns $\operatorname{bin}_n(q)\#\operatorname{bin}_n(r)$, where $q=\lfloor a/b\rfloor$ and $r=a-bq$. The square-root machine takes $x$ and returns $\operatorname{bin}_n(\lfloor\sqrt a\rfloor)$. Leading zeroes are allowed, and $\kappa$ is as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.
  - Explanation: Newton approximation with exact remainder and square tests transfers the multiplication bound to quotient and remainder computation and floor square root.
- **claim-elementary-stream-operations** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-elementary-streams)): Fix a radix $q\ge2$. Each of the following operations on an array of volume $V$ takes $O(V)$ steps on a fixed number of one-dimensional tapes:* 1. *any fixed permutation of the pair of digit values at two specified radix-$q$ positions, whether or not the positions are adjacent;* 2. *moving one specified radix-$q$ digit to another position, preserving the order of the intervening positions;* 3. *splitting on a specified radix-$q$ digit, applying a fixed pointwise map to aligned streams, and merging them back;* 4. *for positive integers $P,R,B$, within each prefix of an array on $[P]\times[R]\times[B]$, splitting whole rows cyclically into a fixed number $W$ of streams, and, when $W$ divides $R$, merging those streams in any fixed order of the row classes.* *The bounds include shape preparation, counter initialization and resets, and returning or clearing the visited portions of work tapes. Their constants may depend on the fixed radix, the fixed pointwise map, and the fixed number of row classes.
  - Explanation: Fixed digit permutations, digit moves, aligned pointwise maps, and cyclic whole-row splitting admit linear bit-volume implementations. Descriptor preparation, counter resets, and workspace cleanup are included.
- **claim-ordered-affine-updates** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-ordered-affine-streams)): Fix a prime $q$, a fixed number of control fields, and finitely many rational coefficients whose denominators are prime to $q$. Whenever a coefficient is used as a unit, also require its numerator to be prime to $q$. Put $Q=q^b$, $b\ge1$. Suppose a target field $y\in[Q]$ follows its control fields, each control used in an offset having $O(b)$ digits. An update $$y\longmapsto y+a(\text{controls})\pmod Q$$ costs $O(V)$ if the offset can be prepared in $b^{O(1)}$ time from the fixed number of control values. In particular, this holds for a fixed rational linear combination of them, specialized modulo $Q$. Multiplication of $y$ by any of the specified rational units, and its inverse, also costs $O(V)$. Arbitrarily long intervening spectator fields and suffix records are permitted.
  - Explanation: A controlled target shift and fixed rational unit scaling cost linear bit volume when the target follows its controls and the offset has polynomial preparation cost in the field width.
- **claim-restored-scalar-bank-exchange** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-motifs)): For arbitrary initial values of its auxiliary wires, a forward invocation changes $y_S$ to $y_S+x_S$ and leaves every other value unchanged. Performing a forward invocation from $X$ to $Y$ at stage 1, the inverse forward schedule from $Y$ to $X$ at stage 2, and a forward invocation from $X$ to $Y$ at stage 3 sends $$(X_a,Y_a)\longmapsto(-Y_a,X_a)$$ and restores every auxiliary value. In the bit construction this is the permutation exchanging $X_a$ and $Y_a$.
  - Explanation: The scheduled forward invocation adds the source bank to the target while restoring arbitrary scratch contents. The composed stages exchange the banks with the prescribed complex sign, which disappears in the bit construction.
- **claim-comparable-residual-labels** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-motif-residuals)): Every edge joins comparable nondegenerate labels. The only decreasing edges are the central-wire edges from time 3 to time 4; their dimension loss is $h$. Consequently, if $c$ is the number of central wires per invocation and $W$ the total number of wires, then $$L=Ich,\qquad \sum_{e}\bigl\|\dim U_{\mathrm{head}(e)}- \dim U_{\mathrm{tail}(e)}\bigr\| =Wm-2N+2L. \label{eq:rank-budget-labels}$$ For the binary label space, every nonzero orthogonal residual of an edge has an orthonormal basis.
  - Explanation: The labels are nested and nondegenerate on each edge. Central-wire decreases account for the entire loss, so telescoping terminal dimensions gives the total absolute variation. Binary residuals admit orthonormal bases.
- **claim-bit-network-rank-deficit** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-bit-motif-interface)): The bit network has $W_{\rm b}$ wires, uses only pointwise XOR gates, and sends its input vector to the permutation $\rho$ of that vector. Its rational matrices are common to all incidences of a gate and obey $$M_{\mathrm{sink}(\rho(w))}-M_{\mathrm{source}(w)}=I_m \quad\text{for every input role }w.$$ The sum of the rational ranks of all edge differences is $$\begin{aligned} s_{\rm b} &=\sum_{e}\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb Q} (M_{\mathrm{head}(e)}-M_{\mathrm{tail}(e)})\\ &=W_{\rm b}m-N+2L_{\rm b} =177176569088785861287000000<W_{\rm b}m. \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The XOR network exchanges roles and supplies the same endpoint shear on every role, including scratch roles. Its edge rank sum has a strict deficit, after accounting for the negative source projections.
- **claim-complex-network-phase-deficit** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-complex-motif-interface)): For each edge of the complex network, write $U,V$ for its tail and head labels. Its phase-frame difference $\mathcal C_V\mathcal C_U^{-1}$ is a product of one translation kernel $aI+bX_v$, or its inverse, per vector in an orthonormal basis of the edge residual. Here $(X_vf)(x)=f(x+v)$. The sum of these basis lengths is $$s_{\rm c}=W_{\rm c}m-2N+2L_{\rm c} =1873807244636671267308000000<W_{\rm c}m.$$ After source and sink diagonal corrections, and undoing the signed bank exchange, telescoping these phase frames gives $C^{\otimes m}$ on every role, including auxiliary roles. The same assertion holds on any positive number of columns by taking tensor products over columns.
  - Explanation: Each edge frame change factors through translation kernels along its orthonormal residual basis. Endpoint corrections and restoration of the signed routing give the forward tensor kernel on every role, with a strict total residual deficit.
- **claim-lower-triangular-factorisation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-lower-lower)): Every rational $m\times m$ matrix $A$ of rank $a$ has a factorization $$A=E_1\Pi E_2,$$ where $E_1,E_2$ are invertible lower triangular rational matrices and $\Pi$ has exactly $a$ entries equal to one, with at most one in each row and column, and all remaining entries zero.
  - Explanation: Ordered elimination factors a rational matrix into lower triangular invertible factors and a partial permutation matrix. The number of pivots equals the rational rank.
- **claim-rank-counted-matrix-shear** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-matrix-shear)): Let $\mathcal A$ be a fixed finite collection of rational $m\times m$ matrices. There is a fixed odd prime $q$ such that the following holds. Let $H=(H_1,\ldots,H_m)$ and $D=(D_1,\ldots,D_m)$ be two ordered groups of fields, every field ranging over $[q^b]$, with every $H_i$ before every $D_j$. For $A\in\mathcal A$ of rational rank $a$, the permutation $$(H,D)\longmapsto(H+AD,D) \quad\text{over }\mathbb Z/q^b\mathbb Z$$ can be performed with exactly $a$ interchanges of pairs of $b$-digit fields and $O(V)$ further work. Every interchange is between an $H$ field and a $D$ field. Spectators between any of the formal fields are allowed.
  - Explanation: A fixed suitable prime makes the rational triangular factors usable modulo its powers. Each partial-permutation pivot uses a single recursive field interchange, while the other coordinate updates are linear scans.
- **claim-power-width-row-interchange** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-power-interchange)): Assume the fixed circuit data satisfy <a href="#eq:finite-shear-contract" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-shear-contract">[eq:finite-shear-contract]</a>, and let $q$ be a prime as in Lemma <a href="#lem:matrix-shear" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:matrix-shear">10</a> for its edge matrices, also avoiding the denominators of every assigned terminal and gate matrix $M$. Choose a rational number $\tau\in(0,1)$ with $s/W<m^\tau$. For every $e=m^k$, $k\ge0$, consider two disjoint contiguous address chunks of $e$ radix-$q$ digits each. Suppose a row field preceding both chunks has length divisible by $W^k$. There may be additional fields before and after the row field, between the chunks, and after them. The chunks can be interchanged in $O(Ve^\tau)$ time, preserving every other address field, on a fixed number of finite-alphabet tapes. The constant is independent of all field lengths and of the data.
  - Explanation: Splitting complete rows among fixed wire roles makes each recursive shear call act on a smaller-volume stream. The strict rank deficit gives a fractional-power width cost, and a depth-first tape schedule charges only local parking and restoration.
- **claim-arbitrary-width-chunk-interchange** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-chunk-swap)): There exist fixed $0<\tau<1$ and one fixed finite-alphabet multitape procedure with the following property. For positive integers $P,G,B$ and $u\ge1$, it transforms an arbitrary bit array on $$[P]\times[2^u]\times[G]\times[2^u]\times[B]$$ by the address permutation $$(p,h,g,d,z)\longmapsto(p,d,g,h,z)$$ in $O(Vu^\tau)$ steps, where $V=PG B\,2^{2u}$. The integers specifying the shape are part of the input. The time includes their processing, all padding and unpadding, and fixed-tape workspace cleanup. In particular, $B=1$ is allowed.
  - Explanation: High digits provide rows, padding supplies divisibility, and a base partition handles general widths. Numerical padding between binary and the fixed radix preserves array order and gives an exact interchange with arbitrary spectators and payloads.
- **claim-packed-rectangle-selected-xor** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle)): Let $R\ge1$, $K\ge6$, $f\ge1$, $L=fK$, and $0\le\rho<K$. Let $N_0,N_1,N_2,N_3$ be positive integers, and let $(\chi_1,\chi_2,\chi_3)$ be a permutation of $(x,y,z)$. Consider $R$-bit records in lexicographic order on $$[N_0]\times[2^L]_{\chi_1}\times[N_1]\times[2^L]_{\chi_2} \times[N_2]\times[2^L]_{\chi_3}\times[N_3].$$ The subscript names the chunk at that position. The input supplies $N_0,\ldots,N_3,R,K,f,\rho$ as a fixed number of canonical binary integers, together with the finite permutation of the chunk names, as in Section <a href="#sec:streams" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:streams">2</a>. Put $$M=2^{3L}\prod_{h=0}^3N_h,\qquad V=MR,\qquad A_{\rm rect}=\lceil\log_2(2V)\rceil.$$ At the selected positions $j_i=\rho+iK$, numbered from the least significant bit, one fixed multitape procedure performs $$y_{j_i}\longleftarrow y_{j_i}\mathbin\oplus x_{j_i} \quad(0\le i<f).$$ It preserves the spectator coordinates, all other chunk bits, and every record payload; in particular, it restores the entire chunk $z$ for every initial value. With $\delta_{\rm bad}=\min\{1,80(f-1)2^{-K}\}$, its time is $$\label{eq:packed-rectangle-time} O\!\left(V(L^\tau+1)+MA_{\rm rect}^3+ \delta_{\rm bad}MA_{\rm rect}(R+A_{\rm rect})\right).$$ When $A_{\rm rect}=O(p)$, $R$ exceeds every fixed polynomial in $p$, $f\le d\le p$, and $K/\log p\to\infty$, this is $O(V(L^\tau+1))$, uniformly over the permitted lengths and payloads in the fixed family.
  - Explanation: Packed controlled rotations realise the selected XORs away from an explicitly bounded exceptional set. Exact extraction, destination-key sorting, and reinsertion repair that set, restore the scratch chunk, and preserve payloads.
- **claim-simultaneous-normalised-layer** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-simultaneous-layer)): Fix $$\tau=\sigma=1-2^{-50},\qquad \beta=\frac12,\qquad C_1=20.$$ Let $c,\epsilon,\lambda,\lambda'$ be fixed positive rational numbers satisfying $$\max\{\tau,\sigma\}<\lambda<1,\qquad \tau(1+c/\beta)<\lambda,\qquad \max\{\lambda,\sigma+\beta(1-\sigma)\}<\lambda'<1, \qquad \epsilon C_1<1.$$ Fix positive constants $a_d\le b_d$ and $a_r\le b_r$, and fix positive constants $C_M,C_{\rm desc},C_w$ with $C_w>1$. For these fixed choices there are a cutoff $p_0$, one fixed finite-alphabet multitape procedure, and an implicit time constant with the following guarantee. The cutoff and time constant may depend on the fixed rational parameters and these constants. For every choice of positive integers $p,d,r$ with $p\ge p_0$ and $$a_dp^\epsilon\le d\le b_dp^\epsilon,\qquad 2^{a_rp/d}\le r\le 2^{b_rp/d},$$ put $K=\lfloor d^c\rfloor$, and let $1\le D\le d$, $P,S\ge1$, and $0\le\rho<K$ be integers. Let $w\ge1$ be an integer component width. The input supplies on a descriptor tape the self-delimiting header $$\mathcal E= \Gamma(p)\Gamma(d)\Gamma(r)\Gamma(D)\Gamma(P)\Gamma(S) \Gamma(K)\Gamma(\rho+1)\Gamma(w),$$ using the integer code from <a href="#eq:integer-descriptor-code" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:integer-descriptor-code">[eq:integer-descriptor-code]</a>. Assume that this header is valid with the stated values and that the accompanying array is in lexicographic order on the complete address set $$[P]\times[2^K]^D\times[S], \qquad M=PS\,2^{KD},$$ with the following record format. For every address $x\in[P]\times[2^K]^D\times[S]$, write its record as $$z(x)=\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}z_{x,j}y^j,$$ stored in coefficient order. For every $x$ and $0\le j<r$, assume $\lvert z_{x,j}\rvert\le1$ and $\Re z_{x,j},\Im z_{x,j}\in2^{-p}\mathbb Z$. Each component is $2^{-p}$ times a signed $w$-bit two’s-complement integer, written most significant bit first; the real component precedes the imaginary component. Require the pointwise bounds $$\log_2(2M)\le C_Mp,\qquad \|\mathcal E\|\le C_{\rm desc}p,\qquad p+2\le w\le C_wp,$$ where $\|\mathcal E\|$ is the header’s bit length. The condition $w\ge p+2$ allows every permitted disk-grid component, including $-1$ and $1$. The logical bit volume is $V=2Mrw=\Theta(Mrp)$.* *On each of the $D$ consecutive $K$-bit address chunks, select bit $\rho$, numbered from the least significant bit, and let $\mathcal H$ be the tensor product of $$H_0=\frac12\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\1&-1\end{pmatrix}$$ on those selected bits. It acts coefficientwise and fixes every unselected address bit and both spectator fields. Let $Q_p$ truncate the real and imaginary parts of every coefficient toward zero to $2^{-p}\mathbb Z$.* *The procedure returns $Q_p(\mathcal H z)$ in the same array, coefficient order, and component format in $O(Vd^{\lambda'})$ time, uniformly over all these choices of $p,d,r,D,P,S,\rho,w$ and $z$. Every returned coefficient belongs to the complex unit disk, and, in the maximum coefficient norm, $$\\|Q_p(\mathcal H z)-\mathcal H z\\|_\infty <\sqrt2\,2^{-p}.$$ The procedure evaluates the entire layer exactly before this final truncation. Put $$C_0=128m\bigl(s_{\rm c}+64(W_{\rm c}+m+1)^3\bigr)^2,$$ where $m,W_{\rm c},s_{\rm c}$ are the fixed constants of Proposition <a href="#prop:complex-motif-interface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:complex-motif-interface">8</a>. The procedure uses at most $p+\lceil C_0d^{C_1}\rceil$ fractional bits and $\lceil C_0d^{C_1}\rceil+3$ integer and sign bits per real component internally; these widths are $O(p)$. The time includes address changes, deterministic repair, row padding and its removal, and fixed-tape workspace cleanup. Its tape count is fixed, and its time constant is independent of the allowed array lengths and coefficient values.
  - Explanation: The complex network batches selected butterfly factors over complete address chunks. Packed basis changes, row splitting, and bounded exact Gaussian-dyadic arithmetic yield the accelerated layer; truncation occurs only after the entire layer is complete.
- **claim-synthetic-character-cancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-synthetic-characters)): If $j$ is an integer, then $$\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\omega_t^{jk} =\begin{cases}t,&t\mid j,\\0,&t\nmid j.\end{cases} \qquad F_t^+F_t^-=t^{-1}I.$$
  - Explanation: A pairing of summands with opposite signs proves character cancellation directly in the quotient ring. The composite of the oppositely signed normalised transforms is the scaled identity.
- **claim-retained-synthetic-transform-layout** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-synthetic-transform-cost)): Fix the rational parameters and positive comparison constants $a_d\leq b_d$, $a_r\leq b_r$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:simultaneous-layer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:simultaneous-layer">14</a>. Fix constants $C_\ell>0$ and $C_w>1$, and choose the layer constants once as $$C_M=C_\ell+1,\qquad C_{\rm desc}=32(C_\ell+C_w+1).$$ Use these constants and $C_w$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:simultaneous-layer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:simultaneous-layer">14</a>. These fixed choices determine a cutoff $p_1$, one fixed-tape procedure, and an implicit time constant with the following guarantee. For every choice of positive integers $p,d,r$ with $p\geq p_1$ and $$a_dp^\epsilon\leq d\leq b_dp^\epsilon,\qquad 2^{a_rp/d}\leq r\leq2^{b_rp/d},$$ suppose $d\geq2$ and $r=2^\ell$ with an integer $2\leq\ell<p$, and require $$d\ell\leq C_\ell p,\qquad 1\leq K=\lfloor d^c\rfloor\leq\ell-1.$$ Let $t_i\in\{2^{\ell-1},2^\ell\}$ for $1\leq i\leq d-1$, put $M=\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}t_i$ and $T=rM$, and let $w$ be an integer with $p+2\leq w\leq C_wp$. The cutoff and time constant are independent of $p,d,r$, these axis choices, the round headers constructed from them, and $w$ within the displayed bounds.* *The input supplies the self-delimiting header $$\mathcal T=\Gamma(p)\Gamma(d)\Gamma(r)\Gamma(K)\Gamma(w) \prod_{i=1}^{d-1}\Gamma(t_i),$$ where the product is concatenation in axis order and $\Gamma$ is the integer code of <a href="#eq:integer-descriptor-code" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:integer-descriptor-code">[eq:integer-descriptor-code]</a>. The supplied $d$ determines the number of axis entries, and $\ell=\log_2r$ is obtained from the supplied power of two $r$. Assume that the header is valid with the stated values and that the input records use the two’s-complement component format of Proposition <a href="#prop:simultaneous-layer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:simultaneous-layer">14</a> with width $w$.* *The procedure constructs an explicit positional layout permutation $L$ of record addresses, preserving their polynomial suffixes, and transforms disk grid arrays of $M$ contiguous degree-less-than-$r$ polynomial records. Its exact counterpart is $LB F_{\boldsymbol t}^-$. Its opposite procedure consumes that order and has exact counterpart $F_{\boldsymbol t}^+B^{-1}L^{-1}$. Each procedure has error less than $\sqrt2\ell\,2^{-p}$, returns disk grid coefficients in the same component format, and costs $$O\left(Tp\left[dK+pK^{\tau-1}+\ell d^{\lambda'}\right]\right). \label{eq:synthetic-transform-cost}$$ Both layout construction and final restoration are charged in this bound. The forward procedure may retain its output layout for a pointwise product and the following opposite procedure.
  - Explanation: A positional bit layout groups participating selected bits without moving polynomial suffixes. The forward transform retains its frequency layout, and the opposite procedure consumes it directly. Contractive rounds accumulate only the stated truncation error.
- **claim-signed-packed-ring-product** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-signed-ring-product)): Let $r=2^\ell<2^p$ with integers $\ell\geq1$ and $p\geq2$, and fix $C_w>1$. Let $w$ be an integer with $p+2\leq w\leq C_wp$. The input supplies $\Gamma(p)\Gamma(r)\Gamma(w)$ and two disk grid polynomials $f,g\in\mathcal R_r$ in the component format of Proposition <a href="#prop:simultaneous-layer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:simultaneous-layer">14</a> with width $w$. One can compute the exact Gaussian integer numerators of $fg$, and then return $Q_p(fg/r)$ in that same format, in time $O(rp\log(rp))$ on a fixed number of tapes. The output is a disk grid polynomial with error less than $\sqrt2\,2^{-p}$ from $fg/r$.
  - Explanation: Signed evaluation integers encode polynomial coefficients without overlap. Exact integer products, centred extraction, and negacyclic wrapping recover the Gaussian numerators; a final normalising shift and truncation returns the disk-grid product.
- **claim-normalised-synthetic-convolution** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-synthetic-convolution)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:synthetic-transform-cost" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:synthetic-transform-cost">16</a>, $\mathcal M_r$ on disk grid operands has an approximation with error less than $$\sqrt2\,M(3\ell+1)2^{-p} \label{eq:synthetic-convolution-error}$$ and cost $$O\left(Tp\left[dK+pK^{\tau-1}+\ell d^{\lambda'} +\log(rp)\right]\right).$$ Put $m_M=\log_2M$ and $w_M=w+m_M$. The returned grid array is in the original array and coefficient order, with the same two’s-complement component format at width $w_M$. The procedure supplies $\Gamma(w_M)$ on the output descriptor tape, so the caller knows every component boundary from this width and the retained shape parameters. If one operand has norm at most $\rho<1$ and the bound in <a href="#eq:synthetic-convolution-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:synthetic-convolution-error">[eq:synthetic-convolution-error]</a> is at most $1-\rho$, the returned grid array is a disk array.
  - Explanation: Forward transforms retain a common record order through normalised polynomial products. The opposite transform and exact final scale recover the normalised convolution with a known output component width and an explicit error margin.
- **claim-no-sort-gaussian-resampling** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-no-sort-resampling)): Fix $0<\delta<1/8$. Let $p>100$, $2\leq s<t<2^p$ and $2\leq\alpha<\sqrt p$ be integers, with $\gcd(s,t)=1$ and $$\theta:=t/s-1>p/\alpha^4.$$ Define coordinate permutations by $$(P_su)_j=u_{tj},\qquad (P_tv)_k=v_{-sk}.$$ There are linear contractions $A:\mathbb C^s\to\mathbb C^t$ and $B_0:\mathbb C^t\to\mathbb C^s$ satisfying $$P_sF_s=2^{2\alpha^2}B_0P_tF_tA. \label{eq:no-sort-one-dimensional}$$ There are fixed multitape algorithms for approximations $\widetilde A:\mathbb D_p^s\to\mathbb D_p^t$ and $\widetilde B_0:\mathbb D_p^t\to\mathbb D_p^s$, each with error less than $p^2$ and time $$O(tp^{3/2+\delta}\alpha).$$ The constant is uniform in $p,s,t,\alpha$. Inputs and outputs use the scalar component format above and occur in increasing coordinate order.
  - Explanation: Gaussian expansion and compression are contractions whose transform identity retains the coordinate permutations. Their ordered approximations therefore avoid separately executing the sorting permutations.
- **claim-tensor-resampling-interface** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor-resampling)): Suppose every pair $(s_i,t_i)$, $1\leq i\leq d$, satisfies Lemma <a href="#lem:no-sort-resampling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:no-sort-resampling">19</a> with the same $p,\alpha,\delta$. Put $T=\prod_i t_i$, $\gamma=2d\alpha^2$, and $$R=\bigotimes_iP_{s_i},\quad Q=\bigotimes_iP_{t_i},\quad \mathcal A=\bigotimes_i A_i,\quad \mathcal B_0=\bigotimes_i B_{0,i}.$$ Then $$RF_{\boldsymbol s} =2^\gamma\mathcal B_0QF_{\boldsymbol t}\mathcal A, \qquad F_{\boldsymbol q}:=\bigotimes_iF_{q_i}. \label{eq:no-sort-tensor}$$ Also write $F_{\boldsymbol q}^+=\bigotimes_iF_{q_i}^+$. Both $\mathcal A$ and $\mathcal B_0$ are contractions. The linewise implementations of $\mathcal A$ and $\mathcal B_0$ each have error less than $dp^2$. The total time spent in the one-dimensional algorithms for both maps is $O(dTp^{3/2+\delta}\alpha)$, with a constant independent of $d$.* *If each $t_i\in\{r/2,r\}$ with $r=2^\ell$, the array is kept in the full padded box of $T$ entries, and each entry occupies $O(p)$ bits, then exposing and restoring all the lines costs $$O\bigl(Tp\,d^2(1+\ell^\tau)\bigr)$$ using the equal-width chunk-swap bound $O(Vu^\tau)$ and the fixed-digit movement primitive. Any fixed polynomial in $p$ of setup per line is negligible compared with $Tp$ when $r$ grows faster than every such polynomial and $d$ is polynomially bounded in $p$.
  - Explanation: Tensoring the resampling identity preserves contraction, and linewise approximation errors add across axes. Keeping the full padded box permits exact axis exposure and restoration by the proved chunk operations.
- **claim-chirp-permutation-cancellation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-chirp-permutation)): With $R,Q,s_i,t_i$ as in Lemma <a href="#lem:tensor-resampling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:tensor-resampling">20</a>, assume every $t_i$ is even. For $\boldsymbol j\in\mathbb Z^d$, define $$a_{\boldsymbol j} =\exp\left(-\pi i\sum_{i=1}^d\frac{s_i j_i^2}{t_i}\right).$$ Then $a$ has period $t_i$ in coordinate $i$, and $$QF_{\boldsymbol t}u =\overline a\mathbin{\cdot} \frac{a*(\overline a\mathbin{\cdot}u)}{T}. \label{eq:permuted-chirp}$$ If $S=\prod_i s_i$, then $$(RF_{\boldsymbol s}^+) \bigl[(RF_{\boldsymbol s}u)\mathbin{\cdot} (RF_{\boldsymbol s}v)\bigr] =\frac{u*v}{S^2}. \label{eq:source-permutation-cancel}$$ Complex conjugation of the input and output of an algorithm for $RF_{\boldsymbol s}$ gives an algorithm for $RF_{\boldsymbol s}^+$.
  - Explanation: The periodic chirp converts the target-permuted transform directly into normalised cyclic convolution. The source permutation cancels through matched forward transforms, pointwise multiplication, and the opposite-sign transform.
- **claim-grouped-packed-selected-xor** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-addition)): Let a valid grouped packed stream of Definition <a href="#def:grouped-packed-stream" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:grouped-packed-stream">22</a> be supplied, and let $A\ge2$ bound the actual total bit length of its descriptor $\mathcal D$, including the header and the entry for $R$. At the positions $j_i=\rho+iK$, numbered from the least significant bit, one fixed multitape procedure performs $$y_{j_i}\longleftarrow y_{j_i}\mathbin\oplus x_{j_i} \quad(0\le i<f)$$ in every group. It fixes the group, row ordinal, spectator coordinates, all other chunk bits, and every record payload; in particular, the chunk $z$ is restored. For $V=MR$, its time is $$\label{eq:packed-selected-time} O\!\left(V(L^\tau+1)+MA^3+ M\min\{1,80(f-1)2^{-K}\}\,A(R+A)\right).$$ When $A=O(p)$, $R$ exceeds every fixed polynomial in $p$, $f\le d\le p$, and $K/\log p\to\infty$, this is $O(V(L^\tau+1))$, uniformly over the group counts, shapes, and payloads whose supplied descriptors satisfy that bound.
  - Explanation: Each complete group is collapsed to the rectangular interface and processed on reused local tapes. Summing local costs yields a descriptor-sensitive bound uniform over group shapes, while preserving row ordinals, spectators, payloads, and scratch contents.

Its rational matrices are common to all incidences of a gate and obey $$M_{\\mathrm{sink}(\\rho(w))}-M_{\\mathrm{source}(w)}=I_m\n \\quad\\text{for every input role }w.$$ The sum of the rational ranks of all edge differences is $$\\begin{aligned}\n s_{\\rm b}\n &=\\sum_{e}\\operatorname{rank}_{\\mathbb Q}\n      (M_{\\mathrm{head}(e)}-M_{\\mathrm{tail}(e)})\\\\\n &=W_{\\rm b}m-N+2L_{\\rm b}\n  =177176569088785861287000000<W_{\\rm b}m.\n\\end{aligned}$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-complex-motif-interface",
        "exact_quote": "For each edge of the complex network, write $U,V$ for its tail and head labels. Its phase-frame difference $\\mathcal C_V\\mathcal C_U^{-1}$ is a product of one translation kernel $aI+bX_v$, or its inverse, per vector in an orthonormal basis of the edge residual. Here $(X_vf)(x)=f(x+v)$. The sum of these basis lengths is $$s_{\\rm c}=W_{\\rm c}m-2N+2L_{\\rm c}\n =1873807244636671267308000000<W_{\\rm c}m.$$ After source and sink diagonal corrections, and undoing the signed bank exchange, telescoping these phase frames gives $C^{\\otimes m}$ on every role, including auxiliary roles.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal A$ be a fixed finite collection of rational $m\\times m$ matrices. There is a fixed odd prime $q$ such that the following holds. For $A\\in\\mathcal A$ of rational rank $a$, the permutation $$(H,D)\\longmapsto(H+AD,D)\n \\quad\\text{over }\\mathbb Z/q^b\\mathbb Z$$ can be performed with exactly $a$ interchanges of pairs of $b$-digit fields and $O(V)$ further work.         "exact_quote": "Assume the fixed circuit data satisfy <a href=\"#eq:finite-shear-contract\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-shear-contract\">[eq:finite-shear-contract]</a>, and let $q$ be a prime as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:matrix-shear\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:matrix-shear\">10</a> for its edge matrices, also avoiding the denominators of every assigned terminal and gate matrix $M$. Choose a rational number $\\tau\\in(0,1)$ with $s/W<m^\\tau$. For every $e=m^k$, $k\\ge0$, consider two disjoint contiguous address chunks of $e$ radix-$q$ digits each. Suppose a row field preceding both chunks has length divisible by $W^k$. There may be additional fields before and after the row field, between the chunks, and after them. The chunks can be interchanged in $O(Ve^\\tau)$ time, preserving every other address field, on a fixed number of finite-alphabet tapes.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R\\ge1$, $K\\ge6$, $f\\ge1$, $L=fK$, and $0\\le\\rho<K$. Let $N_0,N_1,N_2,N_3$ be positive integers, and let $(\\chi_1,\\chi_2,\\chi_3)$ be a permutation of $(x,y,z)$. Consider $R$-bit records in lexicographic order on $$[N_0]\\times[2^L]_{\\chi_1}\\times[N_1]\\times[2^L]_{\\chi_2}\n \\times[N_2]\\times[2^L]_{\\chi_3}\\times[N_3].$$ The subscript names the chunk at that position. The input supplies $N_0,\\ldots,N_3,R,K,f,\\rho$ as a fixed number of canonical binary integers, together with the finite permutation of the chunk names, as in Section <a href=\"#sec:streams\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:streams\">2</a>. Put $$M=2^{3L}\\prod_{h=0}^3N_h,\\qquad V=MR,\\qquad\n A_{\\rm rect}=\\lceil\\log_2(2V)\\rceil.$$ At the selected positions $j_i=\\rho+iK$, numbered from the least significant bit, one fixed multitape procedure performs $$y_{j_i}\\longleftarrow y_{j_i}\\mathbin\\oplus x_{j_i}\n \\quad(0\\le i<f).$$ It preserves the spectator coordinates, all other chunk bits, and every record payload; in particular, it restores the entire chunk $z$ for every initial value. With $\\delta_{\\rm bad}=\\min\\{1,80(f-1)2^{-K}\\}$, its time is $$\\label{eq:packed-rectangle-time}\n O\\!\\left(V(L^\\tau+1)+MA_{\\rm rect}^3+\n       \\delta_{\\rm bad}MA_{\\rm rect}(R+A_{\\rm rect})\\right).$$ When $A_{\\rm rect}=O(p)$, $R$ exceeds every fixed polynomial in $p$, $f\\le d\\le p$, and $K/\\log p\\to\\infty$, this is $O(V(L^\\tau+1))$, uniformly over the permitted lengths and payloads in the fixed family.",
        "source_id": "PM-109.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle",

Consider $R$-bit records in lexicographic order on $$[N_0]\\times[2^L]_{\\chi_1}\\times[N_1]\\times[2^L]_{\\chi_2}\n \\times[N_2]\\times[2^L]_{\\chi_3}\\times[N_3].$$ The subscript names the chunk at that position. The input supplies $N_0,\\ldots,N_3,R,K,f,\\rho$ as a fixed number of canonical binary integers, together with the finite permutation of the chunk names, as in Section <a href=\"#sec:streams\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:streams\">2</a>. Put $$M=2^{3L}\\prod_{h=0}^3N_h,\\qquad V=MR,\\qquad\n A_{\\rm rect}=\\lceil\\log_2(2V)\\rceil.$$ At the selected positions $j_i=\\rho+iK$, numbered from the least significant bit, one fixed multitape procedure performs $$y_{j_i}\\longleftarrow y_{j_i}\\mathbin\\oplus x_{j_i}\n \\quad(0\\le i<f).$$ It preserves the spectator coordinates, all other chunk bits, and every record payload; in particular, it restores the entire chunk $z$ for every initial value.       "exact_statement": "Let $R\\ge1$, $K\\ge6$, $f\\ge1$, $L=fK$, and $0\\le\\rho<K$. Let $N_0,N_1,N_2,N_3$ be positive integers, and let $(\\chi_1,\\chi_2,\\chi_3)$ be a permutation of $(x,y,z)$. Put $$M=2^{3L}\\prod_{h=0}^3N_h,\\qquad V=MR,\\qquad\n A_{\\rm rect}=\\lceil\\log_2(2V)\\rceil.$$ At the selected positions $j_i=\\rho+iK$, numbered from the least significant bit, one fixed multitape procedure performs $$y_{j_i}\\longleftarrow y_{j_i}\\mathbin\\oplus x_{j_i}\n \\quad(0\\le i<f).$$ It preserves the spectator coordinates, all other chunk bits, and every record payload; in particular, it restores the entire chunk $z$ for every initial value. With $\\delta_{\\rm bad}=\\min\\{1,80(f-1)2^{-K}\\}$, its time is $$\\label{eq:packed-rectangle-time}\n O\\!\\left(V(L^\\tau+1)+MA_{\\rm rect}^3+\n       \\delta_{\\rm bad}MA_{\\rm rect}(R+A_{\\rm rect})\\right).$$ When $A_{\\rm rect}=O(p)$, $R$ exceeds every fixed polynomial in $p$, $f\\le d\\le p$, and $K/\\log p\\to\\infty$, this is $O(V(L^\\tau+1))$, uniformly over the permitted lengths and payloads in the fixed family.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R\\ge1$, $K\\ge6$, $f\\ge1$, $L=fK$, and $0\\le\\rho<K$.",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle",
          "exact_quote": "Let $N_0,N_1,N_2,N_3$ be positive integers, and let $(\\chi_1,\\chi_2,\\chi_3)$ be a permutation of $(x,y,z)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-packed-rectangle-selected-xor",
      "kind": "algorithm",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle",
          "exact_quote": "It preserves the spectator coordinates, all other chunk bits, and every record payload; in particular, it restores the entire chunk $z$ for every initial value.",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-simultaneous-layer",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $$\\tau=\\sigma=1-2^{-50},\\qquad \\beta=\\frac12,\\qquad C_1=20.$$ Let $c,\\epsilon,\\lambda,\\lambda'$ be fixed positive rational numbers satisfying $$\\max\\{\\tau,\\sigma\\}<\\lambda<1,\\qquad\n \\tau(1+c/\\beta)<\\lambda,\\qquad\n \\max\\{\\lambda,\\sigma+\\beta(1-\\sigma)\\}<\\lambda'<1,\n \\qquad \\epsilon C_1<1.$$ Fix positive constants $a_d\\le b_d$ and $a_r\\le b_r$, and fix positive constants $C_M,C_{\\rm desc},C_w$ with $C_w>1$. For these fixed choices there are a cutoff $p_0$, one fixed finite-alphabet multitape procedure, and an implicit time constant with the following guarantee. The cutoff and time constant may depend on the fixed rational parameters and these constants. For every choice of positive integers $p,d,r$ with $p\\ge p_0$ and $$a_dp^\\epsilon\\le d\\le b_dp^\\epsilon,\\qquad\n 2^{a_rp/d}\\le r\\le 2^{b_rp/d},$$ put $K=\\lfloor d^c\\rfloor$, and let $1\\le D\\le d$, $P,S\\ge1$, and $0\\le\\rho<K$ be integers. Let $w\\ge1$ be an integer component width. The input supplies on a descriptor tape the self-delimiting header $$\\mathcal E=\n \\Gamma(p)\\Gamma(d)\\Gamma(r)\\Gamma(D)\\Gamma(P)\\Gamma(S)\n \\Gamma(K)\\Gamma(\\rho+1)\\Gamma(w),$$ using the integer code from <a href=\"#eq:integer-descriptor-code\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:integer-descriptor-code\">[eq:integer-descriptor-code]</a>. Assume that this header is valid with the stated values and that the accompanying array is in lexicographic order on the complete address set $$[P]\\times[2^K]^D\\times[S],\n \\qquad M=PS\\,2^{KD},$$ with the following record format. For every address $x\\in[P]\\times[2^K]^D\\times[S]$, write its record as $$z(x)=\\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}z_{x,j}y^j,$$ stored in coefficient order. For every $x$ and $0\\le j<r$, assume $\\lvert z_{x,j}\\rvert\\le1$ and $\\Re z_{x,j},\\Im z_{x,j}\\in2^{-p}\\mathbb Z$. Each component is $2^{-p}$ times a signed $w$-bit two’s-complement integer, written most significant bit first; the real component precedes the imaginary component. Require the pointwise bounds $$\\log_2(2M)\\le C_Mp,\\qquad\n |\\mathcal E|\\le C_{\\rm desc}p,\\qquad\n p+2\\le w\\le C_wp,$$ where $|\\mathcal E|$ is the header’s bit length. The condition $w\\ge p+2$ allows every permitted disk-grid component, including $-1$ and $1$. The logical bit volume is $V=2Mrw=\\Theta(Mrp)$.*\n\n*On each of the $D$ consecutive $K$-bit address chunks, select bit $\\rho$, numbered from the least significant bit, and let $\\mathcal H$ be the tensor product of $$H_0=\\frac12\\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\\\1&-1\\end{pmatrix}$$ on those selected bits. It acts coefficientwise and fixes every unselected address bit and both spectator fields. Let $Q_p$ truncate the real and imaginary parts of every coefficient toward zero to $2^{-p}\\mathbb Z$.*\n\n*The procedure returns $Q_p(\\mathcal H z)$ in the same array, coefficient order, and component format in $O(Vd^{\\lambda'})$ time, uniformly over all these choices of $p,d,r,D,P,S,\\rho,w$ and $z$. Every returned coefficient belongs to the complex unit disk, and, in the maximum coefficient norm, $$\\|Q_p(\\mathcal H z)-\\mathcal H z\\|_\\infty\n       <\\sqrt2\\,2^{-p}.$$ The procedure evaluates the entire layer exactly before this final truncation. Put $$C_0=128m\\bigl(s_{\\rm c}+64(W_{\\rm c}+m+1)^3\\bigr)^2,$$ where $m,W_{\\rm c},s_{\\rm c}$ are the fixed constants of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:complex-motif-interface\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:complex-motif-interface\">8</a>. The procedure uses at most $p+\\lceil C_0d^{C_1}\\rceil$ fractional bits and $\\lceil C_0d^{C_1}\\rceil+3$ integer and sign bits per real component internally; these widths are $O(p)$. The time includes address changes, deterministic repair, row padding and its removal, and fixed-tape workspace cleanup. Its tape count is fixed, and its time constant is independent of the allowed array lengths and coefficient values.",
        "source_id": "PM-109.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-synthetic-characters",
        "exact_quote": "If $j$ is an integer, then $$\\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\\omega_t^{jk}\n =\\begin{cases}t,&t\\mid j,\\\\0,&t\\nmid j.\\end{cases}\n \\qquad F_t^+F_t^-=t^{-1}I.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-109.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-synthetic-characters",
          "exact_quote": "If $j$ is an integer, then $$\\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\\omega_t^{jk}\n =\\begin{cases}t,&t\\mid j,\\\\0,&t\\nmid j.\\end{cases}\n \\qquad F_t^+F_t^-=t^{-1}I.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "If $j$ is an integer, then $$\\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\\omega_t^{jk}\n =\\begin{cases}t,&t\\mid j,\\\\0,&t\\nmid j.\\end{cases}\n \\qquad F_t^+F_t^-=t^{-1}I.$$",
      "explanation": "A pairing of summands with opposite signs proves character cancellation directly in the quotient ring. The composite of the oppositely signed normalised transforms is the scaled identity.",

      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-synthetic-characters",
          "exact_quote": "If $j$ is an integer,",

        },
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-38",
          "exact_quote": "For a power of two $t$ dividing $2r$,",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        }

      "id": "claim-synthetic-character-cancellation",
      "kind": "theorem",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-synthetic-transform-cost",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the rational parameters and positive comparison constants $a_d\\leq b_d$, $a_r\\leq b_r$ of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a>. Fix constants $C_\\ell>0$ and $C_w>1$, and choose the layer constants once as $$C_M=C_\\ell+1,\\qquad\n C_{\\rm desc}=32(C_\\ell+C_w+1).$$ Use these constants and $C_w$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a>. These fixed choices determine a cutoff $p_1$, one fixed-tape procedure, and an implicit time constant with the following guarantee. For every choice of positive integers $p,d,r$ with $p\\geq p_1$ and $$a_dp^\\epsilon\\leq d\\leq b_dp^\\epsilon,\\qquad\n 2^{a_rp/d}\\leq r\\leq2^{b_rp/d},$$ suppose $d\\geq2$ and $r=2^\\ell$ with an integer $2\\leq\\ell<p$, and require $$d\\ell\\leq C_\\ell p,\\qquad\n 1\\leq K=\\lfloor d^c\\rfloor\\leq\\ell-1.$$ Let $t_i\\in\\{2^{\\ell-1},2^\\ell\\}$ for $1\\leq i\\leq d-1$, put $M=\\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}t_i$ and $T=rM$, and let $w$ be an integer with $p+2\\leq w\\leq C_wp$. The cutoff and time constant are independent of $p,d,r$, these axis choices, the round headers constructed from them, and $w$ within the displayed bounds.*\n\n*The input supplies the self-delimiting header $$\\mathcal T=\\Gamma(p)\\Gamma(d)\\Gamma(r)\\Gamma(K)\\Gamma(w)\n       \\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}\\Gamma(t_i),$$ where the product is concatenation in axis order and $\\Gamma$ is the integer code of <a href=\"#eq:integer-descriptor-code\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:integer-descriptor-code\">[eq:integer-descriptor-code]</a>. The supplied $d$ determines the number of axis entries, and $\\ell=\\log_2r$ is obtained from the supplied power of two $r$. Assume that the header is valid with the stated values and that the input records use the two’s-complement component format of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a> with width $w$.*\n\n\n*The procedure constructs an explicit positional layout permutation $L$ of record addresses, preserving their polynomial suffixes, and transforms disk grid arrays of $M$ contiguous degree-less-than-$r$ polynomial records. Its exact counterpart is $LB F_{\\boldsymbol t}^-$. Its opposite procedure consumes that order and has exact counterpart $F_{\\boldsymbol t}^+B^{-1}L^{-1}$. Each procedure has error less than $\\sqrt2\\ell\\,2^{-p}$, returns disk grid coefficients in the same component format, and costs $$O\\left(Tp\\left[dK+pK^{\\tau-1}+\\ell d^{\\lambda'}\\right]\\right).\n \\label{eq:synthetic-transform-cost}$$ Both layout construction and final restoration are charged in this bound. The forward procedure may retain its output layout for a pointwise product and the following opposite procedure.",
        "source_id": "PM-109.1"
      },

Each procedure has error less than $\\sqrt2\\ell\\,2^{-p}$, returns disk grid coefficients in the same component format, and costs $$O\\left(Tp\\left[dK+pK^{\\tau-1}+\\ell d^{\\lambda'}\\right]\\right).\n \\label{eq:synthetic-transform-cost}$$ Both layout construction and final restoration are charged in this bound.       "exact_statement": "Fix the rational parameters and positive comparison constants $a_d\\leq b_d$, $a_r\\leq b_r$ of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a>. Fix constants $C_\\ell>0$ and $C_w>1$, and choose the layer constants once as $$C_M=C_\\ell+1,\\qquad\n C_{\\rm desc}=32(C_\\ell+C_w+1).$$ Use these constants and $C_w$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a>. These fixed choices determine a cutoff $p_1$, one fixed-tape procedure, and an implicit time constant with the following guarantee. For every choice of positive integers $p,d,r$ with $p\\geq p_1$ and $$a_dp^\\epsilon\\leq d\\leq b_dp^\\epsilon,\\qquad\n 2^{a_rp/d}\\leq r\\leq2^{b_rp/d},$$ suppose $d\\geq2$ and $r=2^\\ell$ with an integer $2\\leq\\ell<p$, and require $$d\\ell\\leq C_\\ell p,\\qquad\n 1\\leq K=\\lfloor d^c\\rfloor\\leq\\ell-1.$$ Let $t_i\\in\\{2^{\\ell-1},2^\\ell\\}$ for $1\\leq i\\leq d-1$, put $M=\\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}t_i$ and $T=rM$, and let $w$ be an integer with $p+2\\leq w\\leq C_wp$. The cutoff and time constant are independent of $p,d,r$, these axis choices, the round headers constructed from them, and $w$ within the displayed bounds.*\n\n*The input supplies the self-delimiting header $$\\mathcal T=\\Gamma(p)\\Gamma(d)\\Gamma(r)\\Gamma(K)\\Gamma(w)\n       \\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}\\Gamma(t_i),$$ where the product is concatenation in axis order and $\\Gamma$ is the integer code of <a href=\"#eq:integer-descriptor-code\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:integer-descriptor-code\">[eq:integer-descriptor-code]</a>. The supplied $d$ determines the number of axis entries, and $\\ell=\\log_2r$ is obtained from the supplied power of two $r$. Assume that the header is valid with the stated values and that the input records use the two’s-complement component format of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a> with width $w$.*\n\n\n*The procedure constructs an explicit positional layout permutation $L$ of record addresses, preserving their polynomial suffixes, and transforms disk grid arrays of $M$ contiguous degree-less-than-$r$ polynomial records. Its exact counterpart is $LB F_{\\boldsymbol t}^-$. Its opposite procedure consumes that order and has exact counterpart $F_{\\boldsymbol t}^+B^{-1}L^{-1}$. Each procedure has error less than $\\sqrt2\\ell\\,2^{-p}$, returns disk grid coefficients in the same component format, and costs $$O\\left(Tp\\left[dK+pK^{\\tau-1}+\\ell d^{\\lambda'}\\right]\\right).\n \\label{eq:synthetic-transform-cost}$$ Both layout construction and final restoration are charged in this bound. The forward procedure may retain its output layout for a pointwise product and the following opposite procedure.",
      "explanation": "A positional bit layout groups participating selected bits without moving polynomial suffixes. The forward transform retains its frequency layout, and the opposite procedure consumes it directly. Contractive rounds accumulate only the stated truncation error.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-synthetic-convolution",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:synthetic-transform-cost\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:synthetic-transform-cost\">16</a>, $\\mathcal M_r$ on disk grid operands has an approximation with error less than $$\\sqrt2\\,M(3\\ell+1)2^{-p}\n \\label{eq:synthetic-convolution-error}$$ and cost $$O\\left(Tp\\left[dK+pK^{\\tau-1}+\\ell d^{\\lambda'}\n                    +\\log(rp)\\right]\\right).$$ Put $m_M=\\log_2M$ and $w_M=w+m_M$. The returned grid array is in the original array and coefficient order, with the same two’s-complement component format at width $w_M$. The procedure supplies $\\Gamma(w_M)$ on the output descriptor tape, so the caller knows every component boundary from this width and the retained shape parameters. Inputs and outputs use the scalar component format above and occur in increasing coordinate order.",

        "exact_quote": "Suppose every pair $(s_i,t_i)$, $1\\leq i\\leq d$, satisfies Lemma <a href=\"#lem:no-sort-resampling\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:no-sort-resampling\">19</a> with the same $p,\\alpha,\\delta$. Put $T=\\prod_i t_i$, $\\gamma=2d\\alpha^2$, and $$R=\\bigotimes_iP_{s_i},\\quad Q=\\bigotimes_iP_{t_i},\\quad\n \\mathcal A=\\bigotimes_i A_i,\\quad\n \\mathcal B_0=\\bigotimes_i B_{0,i}.$$ Then $$RF_{\\boldsymbol s}\n   =2^\\gamma\\mathcal B_0QF_{\\boldsymbol t}\\mathcal A,\n \\qquad F_{\\boldsymbol q}:=\\bigotimes_iF_{q_i}.\n \\label{eq:no-sort-tensor}$$ Also write $F_{\\boldsymbol q}^+=\\bigotimes_iF_{q_i}^+$. Both $\\mathcal A$ and $\\mathcal B_0$ are contractions. The linewise implementations of $\\mathcal A$ and $\\mathcal B_0$ each have error less than $dp^2$. The total time spent in the one-dimensional algorithms for both maps is $O(dTp^{3/2+\\delta}\\alpha)$, with a constant independent of $d$.*\n\n*If each $t_i\\in\\{r/2,r\\}$ with $r=2^\\ell$, the array is kept in the full padded box of $T$ entries, and each entry occupies $O(p)$ bits, then exposing and restoring all the lines costs $$O\\bigl(Tp\\,d^2(1+\\ell^\\tau)\\bigr)$$ using the equal-width chunk-swap bound $O(Vu^\\tau)$ and the fixed-digit movement primitive.       "conclusion": {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Fixed finite-alphabet multitape machine** ([source](#sec:streams)): A machine has one fixed finite alphabet and one fixed finite number of one-dimensional tapes. A step reads and writes at most one cell on each tape and moves each head by at most one cell.
- **Binary integer value** ([source](#pm-h-2)): For a binary string $x$ of length $n$, let $\operatorname{val}(x)$ be its nonnegative integer value, with the leftmost bit most significant.
- **Padded binary representation** ([source](#pm-h-2)): For $0\le z<2^k$, let $\operatorname{bin}_k(z)$ denote its binary representation padded on the left to length $k$.
- **Normalised logarithmic notation** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Write $\lg n=\max\{\lceil\log_2 n\rceil,1\}$.
- **Logical bit volume** ([source](#pm-h-12)): The *logical volume* $V$ is the number of stored data bits.
- **Stream row** ([source](#pm-h-12)): If the row field has range $[R]$, a *row* at prefix $p$ and row index $r$ is the complete consecutive block $\{p\}\times\{r\}\times[B]$.
- **Self-delimiting positive-integer descriptor** ([source](#eq:integer-descriptor-code)): For headers that concatenate a list of positive integer parameters, we use the self-delimiting code $$b_v=\lfloor\log_2v\rfloor+1,\qquad \Gamma(v)=0^{b_v-1}\operatorname{bin}_{b_v}(v),\qquad \|\Gamma(v)\|=2b_v-1\quad(v\ge1). \label{eq:integer-descriptor-code}$$
- **Wire role** ([source](#sec:finite-networks)): We call the name of a wire its *role*, so that we can distinguish a fixed place in the network from the value currently carried there.
- **Representation frame** ([source](#eq:common-frame-identity)): At each source, gate and sink $v$, assign an invertible $R$-linear operator $D_v$ on $R^\Omega$, called its *frame*.
- **Common-frame representation invariant** ([source](#eq:common-frame-identity)): At a vertex $v$, the invariant is that the stored array equals $D_v$ applied to the logical array there.
- **Orthogonal edge residual** ([source](#pm-h-16)): For such an edge, the *orthogonal residual* is the orthogonal complement of the smaller label inside the larger one; its dimension is the absolute change in label dimension.
- **Synthetic polynomial ring and coefficient norm** ([source](#eq:transform-truncate)): For an integer $r\geq 2$ that is a power of two, put $$\mathcal R_r=\mathbb C[y]/(y^r+1),\qquad \left\\|\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}z_k y^k\right\\|_\infty =\max_{0\leq k<r}\|z_k\|.$$
- **Componentwise grid truncation** ([source](#eq:transform-truncate)): For any array, let $Q_p$ truncate each real and imaginary coefficient part toward zero to this grid.
- **Disk grid** ([source](#sec:resampling)): For an integer precision $p\geq1$, put $$\mathbb D_p=\{2^{-p}(a+ib):a,b\in\mathbb Z,\ a^2+b^2\leq 2^{2p}\}.$$
- **Normalised synthetic convolution** ([source](#pm-h-41)): For arrays on $\prod_i\mathbb Z/t_i\mathbb Z$ with values in $\mathcal R_r$, let $*$ denote cyclic convolution in the displayed axes and define $$\mathcal M_r(f,g)=\frac{f*g}{rM}=\frac{f*g}{T}.$$
- **Grouped packed stream** ([source](#stmt-def-grouped-packed-stream)): Let $M,R\ge1$, $K\ge6$, $f\ge1$, $L=fK$, and $0\le\rho<K$. Fix an ordered template of $t\ge3$ two-bit tags: $\mathtt{00}$ denotes a spectator field, and $\mathtt{01},\mathtt{10},\mathtt{11}$ denote $x,y,z$, respectively. Each chunk tag occurs exactly once. The template is common to all $J\ge1$ groups of this input, but its length may vary between inputs. Group $\nu$ has a positive row count $n_\nu$ and a positive length $a_{\nu,h}$ at each spectator tag $h$; at a chunk tag put $a_{\nu,h}=2^L$. Define $$Q_\nu=\prod_{h=1}^t a_{\nu,h},\qquad M_\nu=n_\nu Q_\nu,\qquad C_\nu=\sum_{\mu<\nu}M_\mu,\qquad V_\nu=M_\nu R,$$ and require $M=\sum_{\nu=1}^J M_\nu$. The stream consists of $M$ records of $R$ bits. In group order, their addresses form the consecutive lexicographic rectangles $$[n_\nu]\times\prod_{h=1}^t[a_{\nu,h}].$$ The first coordinate is a row ordinal. Every row contains the full Cartesian suffix displayed above: no suffix length depends on a coordinate within that row. The suffix lengths may differ between groups. Every finite sequence of per-row spectator length vectors in the fixed template is representable, using groups of count one when necessary. Use the integer code $\Gamma$ from <a href="#eq:integer-descriptor-code" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:integer-descriptor-code">[eq:integer-descriptor-code]</a>. The supplied descriptor is exactly $$\begin{split} \mathcal D={}& \Gamma(M)\Gamma(R)\Gamma(K)\Gamma(f)\Gamma(\rho+1)\Gamma(t)\, T_1\cdots T_t\,\Gamma(J)\\ &{}\cdot\prod_{\nu=1}^{J} \left(\Gamma(n_\nu) \prod_{h:T_h=\mathtt{00}}\Gamma(a_{\nu,h})\right), \end{split}$$ where $T_h$ is the tag at position $h$, and juxtaposition and products mean bitstring concatenation in increasing index order. The header, tag count, and group count delimit the entire list. A grouped packed stream is valid when this descriptor and its accompanying records have exactly the parameters, shapes, and orders just specified.

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R\\ge1$, $K\\ge6$, $f\\ge1$, $L=fK$, and $0\\le\\rho<K$. Let $N_0,N_1,N_2,N_3$ be positive integers, and let $(\\chi_1,\\chi_2,\\chi_3)$ be a permutation of $(x,y,z)$. Consider $R$-bit records in lexicographic order on $$[N_0]\\times[2^L]_{\\chi_1}\\times[N_1]\\times[2^L]_{\\chi_2}\n \\times[N_2]\\times[2^L]_{\\chi_3}\\times[N_3].$$ The subscript names the chunk at that position. The input supplies $N_0,\\ldots,N_3,R,K,f,\\rho$ as a fixed number of canonical binary integers, together with the finite permutation of the chunk names, as in Section <a href=\"#sec:streams\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:streams\">2</a>. Put $$M=2^{3L}\\prod_{h=0}^3N_h,\\qquad V=MR,\\qquad\n A_{\\rm rect}=\\lceil\\log_2(2V)\\rceil.$$ At the selected positions $j_i=\\rho+iK$, numbered from the least significant bit, one fixed multitape procedure performs $$y_{j_i}\\longleftarrow y_{j_i}\\mathbin\\oplus x_{j_i}\n \\quad(0\\le i<f).$$ It preserves the spectator coordinates, all other chunk bits, and every record payload; in particular, it restores the entire chunk $z$ for every initial value. With $\\delta_{\\rm bad}=\\min\\{1,80(f-1)2^{-K}\\}$, its time is $$\\label{eq:packed-rectangle-time}\n O\\!\\left(V(L^\\tau+1)+MA_{\\rm rect}^3+\n       \\delta_{\\rm bad}MA_{\\rm rect}(R+A_{\\rm rect})\\right).$$ When $A_{\\rm rect}=O(p)$, $R$ exceeds every fixed polynomial in $p$, $f\\le d\\le p$, and $K/\\log p\\to\\infty$, this is $O(V(L^\\tau+1))$, uniformly over the permitted lengths and payloads in the fixed family.",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:packed-bad-fraction",
          "exact_quote": "There are exactly twenty excluded values for each guard in <a href=\"#eq:packed-guard-interval\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:packed-guard-interval\">[eq:packed-guard-interval]</a>. Since its complete range has $2^{K-1}$ values, the union bound over the $2(f-1)$ guards gives $$\\label{eq:packed-bad-fraction}\n |\\mathcal B|\\le\\delta_{\\rm bad}M.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-simultaneous-layer",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $$\\tau=\\sigma=1-2^{-50},\\qquad \\beta=\\frac12,\\qquad C_1=20.$$ Let $c,\\epsilon,\\lambda,\\lambda'$ be fixed positive rational numbers satisfying $$\\max\\{\\tau,\\sigma\\}<\\lambda<1,\\qquad\n \\tau(1+c/\\beta)<\\lambda,\\qquad\n \\max\\{\\lambda,\\sigma+\\beta(1-\\sigma)\\}<\\lambda'<1,\n \\qquad \\epsilon C_1<1.$$ Fix positive constants $a_d\\le b_d$ and $a_r\\le b_r$, and fix positive constants $C_M,C_{\\rm desc},C_w$ with $C_w>1$. For these fixed choices there are a cutoff $p_0$, one fixed finite-alphabet multitape procedure, and an implicit time constant with the following guarantee. The cutoff and time constant may depend on the fixed rational parameters and these constants. For every choice of positive integers $p,d,r$ with $p\\ge p_0$ and $$a_dp^\\epsilon\\le d\\le b_dp^\\epsilon,\\qquad\n 2^{a_rp/d}\\le r\\le 2^{b_rp/d},$$ put $K=\\lfloor d^c\\rfloor$, and let $1\\le D\\le d$, $P,S\\ge1$, and $0\\le\\rho<K$ be integers. Let $w\\ge1$ be an integer component width. The input supplies on a descriptor tape the self-delimiting header $$\\mathcal E=\n \\Gamma(p)\\Gamma(d)\\Gamma(r)\\Gamma(D)\\Gamma(P)\\Gamma(S)\n \\Gamma(K)\\Gamma(\\rho+1)\\Gamma(w),$$ using the integer code from <a href=\"#eq:integer-descriptor-code\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:integer-descriptor-code\">[eq:integer-descriptor-code]</a>. Assume that this header is valid with the stated values and that the accompanying array is in lexicographic order on the complete address set $$[P]\\times[2^K]^D\\times[S],\n \\qquad M=PS\\,2^{KD},$$ with the following record format. For every address $x\\in[P]\\times[2^K]^D\\times[S]$, write its record as $$z(x)=\\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}z_{x,j}y^j,$$ stored in coefficient order. For every $x$ and $0\\le j<r$, assume $\\lvert z_{x,j}\\rvert\\le1$ and $\\Re z_{x,j},\\Im z_{x,j}\\in2^{-p}\\mathbb Z$. Each component is $2^{-p}$ times a signed $w$-bit two’s-complement integer, written most significant bit first; the real component precedes the imaginary component. Require the pointwise bounds $$\\log_2(2M)\\le C_Mp,\\qquad\n |\\mathcal E|\\le C_{\\rm desc}p,\\qquad\n p+2\\le w\\le C_wp,$$ where $|\\mathcal E|$ is the header’s bit length. The condition $w\\ge p+2$ allows every permitted disk-grid component, including $-1$ and $1$. The logical bit volume is $V=2Mrw=\\Theta(Mrp)$.*\n\n*On each of the $D$ consecutive $K$-bit address chunks, select bit $\\rho$, numbered from the least significant bit, and let $\\mathcal H$ be the tensor product of $$H_0=\\frac12\\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\\\1&-1\\end{pmatrix}$$ on those selected bits. It acts coefficientwise and fixes every unselected address bit and both spectator fields. Let $Q_p$ truncate the real and imaginary parts of every coefficient toward zero to $2^{-p}\\mathbb Z$.*\n\n*The procedure returns $Q_p(\\mathcal H z)$ in the same array, coefficient order, and component format in $O(Vd^{\\lambda'})$ time, uniformly over all these choices of $p,d,r,D,P,S,\\rho,w$ and $z$. Every returned coefficient belongs to the complex unit disk, and, in the maximum coefficient norm, $$\\|Q_p(\\mathcal H z)-\\mathcal H z\\|_\\infty\n       <\\sqrt2\\,2^{-p}.$$ The procedure evaluates the entire layer exactly before this final truncation. Put $$C_0=128m\\bigl(s_{\\rm c}+64(W_{\\rm c}+m+1)^3\\bigr)^2,$$ where $m,W_{\\rm c},s_{\\rm c}$ are the fixed constants of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:complex-motif-interface\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:complex-motif-interface\">8</a>. The procedure uses at most $p+\\lceil C_0d^{C_1}\\rceil$ fractional bits and $\\lceil C_0d^{C_1}\\rceil+3$ integer and sign bits per real component internally; these widths are $O(p)$. The time includes address changes, deterministic repair, row padding and its removal, and fixed-tape workspace cleanup. Its tape count is fixed, and its time constant is independent of the allowed array lengths and coefficient values.",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-synthetic-transform-cost",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the rational parameters and positive comparison constants $a_d\\leq b_d$, $a_r\\leq b_r$ of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a>. Fix constants $C_\\ell>0$ and $C_w>1$, and choose the layer constants once as $$C_M=C_\\ell+1,\\qquad\n C_{\\rm desc}=32(C_\\ell+C_w+1).$$ Use these constants and $C_w$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a>. These fixed choices determine a cutoff $p_1$, one fixed-tape procedure, and an implicit time constant with the following guarantee. For every choice of positive integers $p,d,r$ with $p\\geq p_1$ and $$a_dp^\\epsilon\\leq d\\leq b_dp^\\epsilon,\\qquad\n 2^{a_rp/d}\\leq r\\leq2^{b_rp/d},$$ suppose $d\\geq2$ and $r=2^\\ell$ with an integer $2\\leq\\ell<p$, and require $$d\\ell\\leq C_\\ell p,\\qquad\n 1\\leq K=\\lfloor d^c\\rfloor\\leq\\ell-1.$$ Let $t_i\\in\\{2^{\\ell-1},2^\\ell\\}$ for $1\\leq i\\leq d-1$, put $M=\\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}t_i$ and $T=rM$, and let $w$ be an integer with $p+2\\leq w\\leq C_wp$. The cutoff and time constant are independent of $p,d,r$, these axis choices, the round headers constructed from them, and $w$ within the displayed bounds.*\n\n*The input supplies the self-delimiting header $$\\mathcal T=\\Gamma(p)\\Gamma(d)\\Gamma(r)\\Gamma(K)\\Gamma(w)\n       \\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}\\Gamma(t_i),$$ where the product is concatenation in axis order and $\\Gamma$ is the integer code of <a href=\"#eq:integer-descriptor-code\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:integer-descriptor-code\">[eq:integer-descriptor-code]</a>. The supplied $d$ determines the number of axis entries, and $\\ell=\\log_2r$ is obtained from the supplied power of two $r$. Assume that the header is valid with the stated values and that the input records use the two’s-complement component format of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a> with width $w$.*\n\n\n*The procedure constructs an explicit positional layout permutation $L$ of record addresses, preserving their polynomial suffixes, and transforms disk grid arrays of $M$ contiguous degree-less-than-$r$ polynomial records. Its exact counterpart is $LB F_{\\boldsymbol t}^-$. Its opposite procedure consumes that order and has exact counterpart $F_{\\boldsymbol t}^+B^{-1}L^{-1}$. Each procedure has error less than $\\sqrt2\\ell\\,2^{-p}$, returns disk grid coefficients in the same component format, and costs $$O\\left(Tp\\left[dK+pK^{\\tau-1}+\\ell d^{\\lambda'}\\right]\\right).\n \\label{eq:synthetic-transform-cost}$$ Both layout construction and final restoration are charged in this bound. The forward procedure may retain its output layout for a pointwise product and the following opposite procedure.",

Put $T=\\prod_i t_i$, $\\gamma=2d\\alpha^2$, and $$R=\\bigotimes_iP_{s_i},\\quad Q=\\bigotimes_iP_{t_i},\\quad\n \\mathcal A=\\bigotimes_i A_i,\\quad\n \\mathcal B_0=\\bigotimes_i B_{0,i}.$$ Then $$RF_{\\boldsymbol s}\n   =2^\\gamma\\mathcal B_0QF_{\\boldsymbol t}\\mathcal A,\n \\qquad F_{\\boldsymbol q}:=\\bigotimes_iF_{q_i}.\n \\label{eq:no-sort-tensor}$$ Also write $F_{\\boldsymbol q}^+=\\bigotimes_iF_{q_i}^+$. Both $\\mathcal A$ and $\\mathcal B_0$ are contractions. The linewise implementations of $\\mathcal A$ and $\\mathcal B_0$ each have error less than $dp^2$. The total time spent in the one-dimensional algorithms for both maps is $O(dTp^{3/2+\\delta}\\alpha)$, with a constant independent of $d$.*\n\n*If each $t_i\\in\\{r/2,r\\}$ with $r=2^\\ell$, the array is kept in the full padded box of $T$ entries, and each entry occupies $O(p)$ bits, then exposing and restoring all the lines costs $$O\\bigl(Tp\\,d^2(1+\\ell^\\tau)\\bigr)$$ using the equal-width chunk-swap bound $O(Vu^\\tau)$ and the fixed-digit movement primitive. Any fixed polynomial in $p$ of setup per line is negligible compared with $Tp$ when $r$ grows faster than every such polynomial and $d$ is polynomially bounded in $p$.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-elementary-streams** [claim](#stmt-lem-elementary-streams) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-elementary-streams): Split by fixed digits, scan aligned streams, and merge in prescribed order; amortise local ripple counters and charge every rewind. -> Linear-volume elementary stream primitives.
- **step-ordered-affine-streams** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordered-affine-streams) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordered-affine-streams): Rotate consecutive target fibers and implement fixed unit scalings by split-and-merge scans. -> Linear-volume controlled shifts and rational unit scalings.
- **step-restored-motifs** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-motifs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-motifs): Subtract old scratch contributions, inject source contributions, apply the intersection pairings, and reverse scratch updates. -> Signed bank exchange with every auxiliary value restored.
- **step-residual-labels** [claim](#stmt-lem-motif-residuals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-motif-residuals): Check nested orthogonal decompositions on all data and scratch edges, telescope signed dimensions, and construct orthonormal residual bases. -> Exact residual budget and identified decreasing edges.
- **step-bit-rank-interface** [claim](#stmt-prop-bit-motif-interface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bit-motif-interface): Assign rational projections, include the negative source projection ranks, and calculate the endpoint differences on routed roles. -> Uniform endpoint shear and strict total rational-rank deficit.
- **step-complex-phase-interface** [claim](#stmt-prop-complex-motif-interface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-complex-motif-interface): Use orthogonal weight additivity to factor phase differences into translation kernels and apply endpoint phase and routing corrections. -> Forward tensor kernel on every role with a strict residual deficit.
- **step-lower-triangular-elimination** [claim](#stmt-lem-lower-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lower-lower): Pivot in the topmost active nonzero row at its rightmost nonzero entry and eliminate using lower triangular operations. -> Lower triangular factors and a rank-sized partial permutation.
- **step-rank-counted-shear** [claim](#stmt-lem-matrix-shear) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-matrix-shear): Compile a suitable prime and triangular factor table, then implement each partial-permutation pivot with a field interchange and ordered updates. -> Matrix shear with interchange count equal to rational rank.
- **step-power-width-interchange** [claim](#stmt-prop-power-interchange) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-power-interchange): Split rows among roles, execute recursive edge shears, telescope frames, undo role routing, and solve the volume-normalised recurrence. -> Accelerated exact interchange with a fixed-tape depth-first schedule.
- **step-arbitrary-width-interchange** [claim](#stmt-lem-chunk-swap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chunk-swap): Reserve high digits as rows, pad divisibility, partition the remaining width, and numerically pad binary ranges into the fixed radix. -> Exact chunk interchange including padding, descriptors, and cleanup.
- **step-packed-rectangle-xor** [claim](#stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle): Compose packed cyclic shifts, identify guard exceptions, and repair only exceptional records using exact destination ranks. -> Selected XOR additions preserving scratch, spectators, and payloads.
- **step-simultaneous-layer** [claim](#stmt-prop-simultaneous-layer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-simultaneous-layer): Implement residual basis changes with packed XORs, split whole rows among roles, unroll the recursive layer cost, and reserve exact denominator and magnitude guards. -> Exact completed normalised layer followed by a single truncation.
- **step-synthetic-characters** [claim](#stmt-lem-synthetic-characters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-synthetic-characters): Pair nontrivial character summands by a translation that negates them, then expand the composite transforms. -> Character cancellation and exact opposite-transform normalisation.
- **step-transform-layout** [claim](#stmt-lem-synthetic-transform-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-synthetic-transform-cost): Move named prefix slots, group equal-width chunks, execute contractive transform rounds, and retain the forward frequency layout for the opposite procedure. -> Forward and opposite transforms with charged layouts and bounded accumulated error.
- **step-signed-ring-product** [claim](#stmt-lem-signed-ring-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-signed-ring-product): Form signed evaluation integers, multiply magnitudes exactly, extract centred coefficients with carries, wrap negacyclically, and truncate the normalised result. -> Exact Gaussian numerators and a normalised disk-grid product.
- **step-synthetic-convolution** [claim](#stmt-prop-synthetic-convolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-synthetic-convolution): Compute matched forward transforms, multiply records in retained order, consume that order with the opposite transform, and apply the exact final scale. -> Normalised synthetic convolution with explicit error and output width.
- **step-ordered-gaussian-resampling** [claim](#stmt-lem-no-sort-resampling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-sort-resampling): Use the Gaussian identity and Neumann inverse estimate, retain both permutations, and adapt ordered arithmetic maps with value-preserving record conversions. -> Ordered expansion and compression contractions with uniform approximation bounds.
- **step-tensor-resampling** [claim](#stmt-lem-tensor-resampling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tensor-resampling): Tensor the identities, expand or compress valid coordinate lines in the padded box, and expose axes by exact chunk moves. -> Tensor contractions, additive linewise errors, and charged axis movement.
- **step-chirp-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-lem-chirp-permutation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chirp-permutation): Expand chirp coefficients to identify the target-permuted transform and commute source dilation through pointwise products and the opposite transform. -> Chirp convolution identity and cancellation of retained source permutations.
- **step-exact-multiplication-assembly** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:assembly): Choose fixed rational parameters and deterministic prime axes, implement triangular coefficient addressing, compose resampling and twisted convolution, reverse both normalisations, round exact coefficients, and propagate carries. Sum all charged costs and handle bounded lengths directly. -> A single fixed machine producing the exact padded product with the claimed worst-case bound.
- **step-exact-division-square-root** [claim](#stmt-cor-exact-arithmetic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chirp-permutation-2): Use guarded capped precision doubling for reciprocal and root iterates, bound the multiplication costs by a geometric precision sum, and correct final trial answers with exact remainder and square tests. -> Exact quotient and remainder or floor square root in the same asymptotic time.
- **step-grouped-packed-stream** [claim](#stmt-def-grouped-packed-stream) / [proof](#stmt-def-grouped-packed-stream): Specify the shared chunk-tag template, complete per-group rectangles, and self-delimiting descriptor order. -> A valid grouped packed-stream interface.
- **step-grouped-packed-xor** [claim](#stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-addition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-addition): Parse each group, collapse spectator intervals, apply the rectangular algorithm locally, and sum descriptor-sensitive costs while reusing fixed tapes. -> Uniform grouped selected-bit addition preserving group boundaries and payloads.

          "exact_quote": "There exist fixed $0<\\tau<1$ and one fixed finite-alphabet multitape procedure with the following property. The integers specifying the shape are part of the input. The time includes their processing, all padding and unpadding, and fixed-tape workspace cleanup. In particular, $B=1$ is allowed.",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-arbitrary-width-interchange",
        "step-ordered-affine-streams",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R\\ge1$, $K\\ge6$, $f\\ge1$, $L=fK$, and $0\\le\\rho<K$. Let $N_0,N_1,N_2,N_3$ be positive integers, and let $(\\chi_1,\\chi_2,\\chi_3)$ be a permutation of $(x,y,z)$. Consider $R$-bit records in lexicographic order on $$[N_0]\\times[2^L]_{\\chi_1}\\times[N_1]\\times[2^L]_{\\chi_2}\n \\times[N_2]\\times[2^L]_{\\chi_3}\\times[N_3].$$ The subscript names the chunk at that position. The input supplies $N_0,\\ldots,N_3,R,K,f,\\rho$ as a fixed number of canonical binary integers, together with the finite permutation of the chunk names, as in Section <a href=\"#sec:streams\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:streams\">2</a>. Put $$M=2^{3L}\\prod_{h=0}^3N_h,\\qquad V=MR,\\qquad\n A_{\\rm rect}=\\lceil\\log_2(2V)\\rceil.$$ At the selected positions $j_i=\\rho+iK$, numbered from the least significant bit, one fixed multitape procedure performs $$y_{j_i}\\longleftarrow y_{j_i}\\mathbin\\oplus x_{j_i}\n \\quad(0\\le i<f).$$ It preserves the spectator coordinates, all other chunk bits, and every record payload; in particular, it restores the entire chunk $z$ for every initial value. With $\\delta_{\\rm bad}=\\min\\{1,80(f-1)2^{-K}\\}$, its time is $$\\label{eq:packed-rectangle-time}\n O\\!\\left(V(L^\\tau+1)+MA_{\\rm rect}^3+\n       \\delta_{\\rm bad}MA_{\\rm rect}(R+A_{\\rm rect})\\right).$$ When $A_{\\rm rect}=O(p)$, $R$ exceeds every fixed polynomial in $p$, $f\\le d\\le p$, and $K/\\log p\\to\\infty$, this is $O(V(L^\\tau+1))$, uniformly over the permitted lengths and payloads in the fixed family.",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-packed-rectangle-xor",
      "inputs": [
        "Complete rectangular chunk ranges",
        "Selected-bit spacing",
        "Arbitrary scratch chunk and payloads"
      ],
      "method": "Compose packed cyclic shifts, identify guard exceptions, and repair only exceptional records using exact destination ranks.",
      "output": "Selected XOR additions preserving scratch, spectators, and payloads.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-packed-selected-bit-rectangle"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-simultaneous-layer",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-complex-phase-interface",
        "step-packed-rectangle-xor",
