      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-simultaneous-layer",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $$\\tau=\\sigma=1-2^{-50},\\qquad \\beta=\\frac12,\\qquad C_1=20.$$ Let $c,\\epsilon,\\lambda,\\lambda'$ be fixed positive rational numbers satisfying $$\\max\\{\\tau,\\sigma\\}<\\lambda<1,\\qquad\n \\tau(1+c/\\beta)<\\lambda,\\qquad\n \\max\\{\\lambda,\\sigma+\\beta(1-\\sigma)\\}<\\lambda'<1,\n \\qquad \\epsilon C_1<1.$$ Fix positive constants $a_d\\le b_d$ and $a_r\\le b_r$, and fix positive constants $C_M,C_{\\rm desc},C_w$ with $C_w>1$. For these fixed choices there are a cutoff $p_0$, one fixed finite-alphabet multitape procedure, and an implicit time constant with the following guarantee. The cutoff and time constant may depend on the fixed rational parameters and these constants. For every choice of positive integers $p,d,r$ with $p\\ge p_0$ and $$a_dp^\\epsilon\\le d\\le b_dp^\\epsilon,\\qquad\n 2^{a_rp/d}\\le r\\le 2^{b_rp/d},$$ put $K=\\lfloor d^c\\rfloor$, and let $1\\le D\\le d$, $P,S\\ge1$, and $0\\le\\rho<K$ be integers. Let $w\\ge1$ be an integer component width. The input supplies on a descriptor tape the self-delimiting header $$\\mathcal E=\n \\Gamma(p)\\Gamma(d)\\Gamma(r)\\Gamma(D)\\Gamma(P)\\Gamma(S)\n \\Gamma(K)\\Gamma(\\rho+1)\\Gamma(w),$$ using the integer code from <a href=\"#eq:integer-descriptor-code\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:integer-descriptor-code\">[eq:integer-descriptor-code]</a>. Assume that this header is valid with the stated values and that the accompanying array is in lexicographic order on the complete address set $$[P]\\times[2^K]^D\\times[S],\n \\qquad M=PS\\,2^{KD},$$ with the following record format. For every address $x\\in[P]\\times[2^K]^D\\times[S]$, write its record as $$z(x)=\\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}z_{x,j}y^j,$$ stored in coefficient order. For every $x$ and $0\\le j<r$, assume $\\lvert z_{x,j}\\rvert\\le1$ and $\\Re z_{x,j},\\Im z_{x,j}\\in2^{-p}\\mathbb Z$. Each component is $2^{-p}$ times a signed $w$-bit two’s-complement integer, written most significant bit first; the real component precedes the imaginary component. Require the pointwise bounds $$\\log_2(2M)\\le C_Mp,\\qquad\n |\\mathcal E|\\le C_{\\rm desc}p,\\qquad\n p+2\\le w\\le C_wp,$$ where $|\\mathcal E|$ is the header’s bit length. The condition $w\\ge p+2$ allows every permitted disk-grid component, including $-1$ and $1$. The logical bit volume is $V=2Mrw=\\Theta(Mrp)$.*\n\n*On each of the $D$ consecutive $K$-bit address chunks, select bit $\\rho$, numbered from the least significant bit, and let $\\mathcal H$ be the tensor product of $$H_0=\\frac12\\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\\\1&-1\\end{pmatrix}$$ on those selected bits. It acts coefficientwise and fixes every unselected address bit and both spectator fields. Let $Q_p$ truncate the real and imaginary parts of every coefficient toward zero to $2^{-p}\\mathbb Z$.*\n\n*The procedure returns $Q_p(\\mathcal H z)$ in the same array, coefficient order, and component format in $O(Vd^{\\lambda'})$ time, uniformly over all these choices of $p,d,r,D,P,S,\\rho,w$ and $z$. Every returned coefficient belongs to the complex unit disk, and, in the maximum coefficient norm, $$\\|Q_p(\\mathcal H z)-\\mathcal H z\\|_\\infty\n       <\\sqrt2\\,2^{-p}.$$ The procedure evaluates the entire layer exactly before this final truncation. Put $$C_0=128m\\bigl(s_{\\rm c}+64(W_{\\rm c}+m+1)^3\\bigr)^2,$$ where $m,W_{\\rm c},s_{\\rm c}$ are the fixed constants of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:complex-motif-interface\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:complex-motif-interface\">8</a>. The procedure uses at most $p+\\lceil C_0d^{C_1}\\rceil$ fractional bits and $\\lceil C_0d^{C_1}\\rceil+3$ integer and sign bits per real component internally; these widths are $O(p)$. The time includes address changes, deterministic repair, row padding and its removal, and fixed-tape workspace cleanup. Its tape count is fixed, and its time constant is independent of the allowed array lengths and coefficient values.",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-synthetic-transform-cost",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the rational parameters and positive comparison constants $a_d\\leq b_d$, $a_r\\leq b_r$ of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a>. Fix constants $C_\\ell>0$ and $C_w>1$, and choose the layer constants once as $$C_M=C_\\ell+1,\\qquad\n C_{\\rm desc}=32(C_\\ell+C_w+1).$$ Use these constants and $C_w$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a>. These fixed choices determine a cutoff $p_1$, one fixed-tape procedure, and an implicit time constant with the following guarantee. For every choice of positive integers $p,d,r$ with $p\\geq p_1$ and $$a_dp^\\epsilon\\leq d\\leq b_dp^\\epsilon,\\qquad\n 2^{a_rp/d}\\leq r\\leq2^{b_rp/d},$$ suppose $d\\geq2$ and $r=2^\\ell$ with an integer $2\\leq\\ell<p$, and require $$d\\ell\\leq C_\\ell p,\\qquad\n 1\\leq K=\\lfloor d^c\\rfloor\\leq\\ell-1.$$ Let $t_i\\in\\{2^{\\ell-1},2^\\ell\\}$ for $1\\leq i\\leq d-1$, put $M=\\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}t_i$ and $T=rM$, and let $w$ be an integer with $p+2\\leq w\\leq C_wp$. The cutoff and time constant are independent of $p,d,r$, these axis choices, the round headers constructed from them, and $w$ within the displayed bounds.*\n\n*The input supplies the self-delimiting header $$\\mathcal T=\\Gamma(p)\\Gamma(d)\\Gamma(r)\\Gamma(K)\\Gamma(w)\n       \\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}\\Gamma(t_i),$$ where the product is concatenation in axis order and $\\Gamma$ is the integer code of <a href=\"#eq:integer-descriptor-code\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:integer-descriptor-code\">[eq:integer-descriptor-code]</a>. The supplied $d$ determines the number of axis entries, and $\\ell=\\log_2r$ is obtained from the supplied power of two $r$. Assume that the header is valid with the stated values and that the input records use the two’s-complement component format of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a> with width $w$.*\n\n\n*The procedure constructs an explicit positional layout permutation $L$ of record addresses, preserving their polynomial suffixes, and transforms disk grid arrays of $M$ contiguous degree-less-than-$r$ polynomial records. Its exact counterpart is $LB F_{\\boldsymbol t}^-$. Its opposite procedure consumes that order and has exact counterpart $F_{\\boldsymbol t}^+B^{-1}L^{-1}$. Each procedure has error less than $\\sqrt2\\ell\\,2^{-p}$, returns disk grid coefficients in the same component format, and costs $$O\\left(Tp\\left[dK+pK^{\\tau-1}+\\ell d^{\\lambda'}\\right]\\right).\n \\label{eq:synthetic-transform-cost}$$ Both layout construction and final restoration are charged in this bound. The forward procedure may retain its output layout for a pointwise product and the following opposite procedure.",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        }

The input supplies $\\Gamma(p)\\Gamma(r)\\Gamma(w)$ and two disk grid polynomials $f,g\\in\\mathcal R_r$ in the component format of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:simultaneous-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:simultaneous-layer\">14</a> with width $w$.           "anchor": "stmt-prop-synthetic-convolution",

The returned grid array is in the original array and coefficient order, with the same two’s-complement component format at width $w_M$.           "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\delta<1/8$. Let $p>100$, $2\\leq s<t<2^p$ and $2\\leq\\alpha<\\sqrt p$ be integers, with $\\gcd(s,t)=1$ and $$\\theta:=t/s-1>p/\\alpha^4.$$ Define coordinate permutations by $$(P_su)_j=u_{tj},\\qquad (P_tv)_k=v_{-sk}.$$ There are linear contractions $A:\\mathbb C^s\\to\\mathbb C^t$ and $B_0:\\mathbb C^t\\to\\mathbb C^s$ satisfying $$P_sF_s=2^{2\\alpha^2}B_0P_tF_tA.\n \\label{eq:no-sort-one-dimensional}$$ There are fixed multitape algorithms for approximations $\\widetilde A:\\mathbb D_p^s\\to\\mathbb D_p^t$ and $\\widetilde B_0:\\mathbb D_p^t\\to\\mathbb D_p^s$, each with error less than $p^2$ and time $$O(tp^{3/2+\\delta}\\alpha).$$ The constant is uniform in $p,s,t,\\alpha$.       "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-tensor-resampling",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-tensor-resampling",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose every pair $(s_i,t_i)$, $1\\leq i\\leq d$, satisfies Lemma <a href=\"#lem:no-sort-resampling\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:no-sort-resampling\">19</a> with the same $p,\\alpha,\\delta$. Put $T=\\prod_i t_i$, $\\gamma=2d\\alpha^2$, and $$R=\\bigotimes_iP_{s_i},\\quad Q=\\bigotimes_iP_{t_i},\\quad\n \\mathcal A=\\bigotimes_i A_i,\\quad\n \\mathcal B_0=\\bigotimes_i B_{0,i}.$$ Then $$RF_{\\boldsymbol s}\n   =2^\\gamma\\mathcal B_0QF_{\\boldsymbol t}\\mathcal A,\n \\qquad F_{\\boldsymbol q}:=\\bigotimes_iF_{q_i}.\n \\label{eq:no-sort-tensor}$$ Also write $F_{\\boldsymbol q}^+=\\bigotimes_iF_{q_i}^+$. Both $\\mathcal A$ and $\\mathcal B_0$ are contractions. The linewise implementations of $\\mathcal A$ and $\\mathcal B_0$ each have error less than $dp^2$. The total time spent in the one-dimensional algorithms for both maps is $O(dTp^{3/2+\\delta}\\alpha)$, with a constant independent of $d$.*\n\n*If each $t_i\\in\\{r/2,r\\}$ with $r=2^\\ell$, the array is kept in the full padded box of $T$ entries, and each entry occupies $O(p)$ bits, then exposing and restoring all the lines costs $$O\\bigl(Tp\\,d^2(1+\\ell^\\tau)\\bigr)$$ using the equal-width chunk-swap bound $O(Vu^\\tau)$ and the fixed-digit movement primitive. Any fixed polynomial in $p$ of setup per line is negligible compared with $Tp$ when $r$ grows faster than every such polynomial and $d$ is polynomially bounded in $p$.",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-chirp-permutation",
          "exact_quote": "With $R,Q,s_i,t_i$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:tensor-resampling\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:tensor-resampling\">20</a>, assume every $t_i$ is even. For $\\boldsymbol j\\in\\mathbb Z^d$, define $$a_{\\boldsymbol j}\n =\\exp\\left(-\\pi i\\sum_{i=1}^d\\frac{s_i j_i^2}{t_i}\\right).$$ Then $a$ has period $t_i$ in coordinate $i$, and $$QF_{\\boldsymbol t}u\n =\\overline a\\mathbin{\\cdot}\n       \\frac{a*(\\overline a\\mathbin{\\cdot}u)}{T}.\n \\label{eq:permuted-chirp}$$ If $S=\\prod_i s_i$, then $$(RF_{\\boldsymbol s}^+)\n   \\bigl[(RF_{\\boldsymbol s}u)\\mathbin{\\cdot}\n         (RF_{\\boldsymbol s}v)\\bigr]\n =\\frac{u*v}{S^2}.\n \\label{eq:source-permutation-cancel}$$ Complex conjugation of the input and output of an algorithm for $RF_{\\boldsymbol s}$ gives an algorithm for $RF_{\\boldsymbol s}^+$.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-exact-arithmetic",
          "exact_quote": "There are deterministic machines with fixed finite alphabets and fixed finite numbers of one-dimensional tapes performing the following tasks in $O(n(\\lg n)^{1-\\kappa})$ worst-case time, for every $n\\ge1$. For $x,y\\in\\{0,1\\}^n$, put $a=\\operatorname{val}(x)$ and $b=\\operatorname{val}(y)$. If $b>0$, the division machine takes $x\\#y$ and returns $\\operatorname{bin}_n(q)\\#\\operatorname{bin}_n(r)$, where $q=\\lfloor a/b\\rfloor$ and $r=a-bq$. The square-root machine takes $x$ and returns $\\operatorname{bin}_n(\\lfloor\\sqrt a\\rfloor)$. Leading zeroes are allowed, and $\\kappa$ is as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-109.1"
        }
      ],

      "method": "Use guarded capped precision doubling for reciprocal and root iterates, bound the multiplication costs by a geometric precision sum, and correct final trial answers with exact remainder and square tests.",
      "output": "Exact quotient and remainder or floor square root in the same asymptotic time.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-def-grouped-packed-stream",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-grouped-packed-stream",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M,R\\ge1$, $K\\ge6$, $f\\ge1$, $L=fK$, and $0\\le\\rho<K$. Fix an ordered template of $t\\ge3$ two-bit tags: $\\mathtt{00}$ denotes a spectator field, and $\\mathtt{01},\\mathtt{10},\\mathtt{11}$ denote $x,y,z$, respectively. Group $\\nu$ has a positive row count $n_\\nu$ and a positive length $a_{\\nu,h}$ at each spectator tag $h$; at a chunk tag put $a_{\\nu,h}=2^L$. Define $$Q_\\nu=\\prod_{h=1}^t a_{\\nu,h},\\qquad\n M_\\nu=n_\\nu Q_\\nu,\\qquad\n C_\\nu=\\sum_{\\mu<\\nu}M_\\mu,\\qquad V_\\nu=M_\\nu R,$$ and require $M=\\sum_{\\nu=1}^J M_\\nu$.\n\nThe stream consists of $M$ records of $R$ bits. In group order, their addresses form the consecutive lexicographic rectangles $$[n_\\nu]\\times\\prod_{h=1}^t[a_{\\nu,h}].$$ The first coordinate is a row ordinal. Every finite sequence of per-row spectator length vectors in the fixed template is representable, using groups of count one when necessary.\n\nUse the integer code $\\Gamma$ from <a href=\"#eq:integer-descriptor-code\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:integer-descriptor-code\">[eq:integer-descriptor-code]</a>. The supplied descriptor is exactly $$\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal D={}&\n \\Gamma(M)\\Gamma(R)\\Gamma(K)\\Gamma(f)\\Gamma(\\rho+1)\\Gamma(t)\\,\n T_1\\cdots T_t\\,\\Gamma(J)\\\\\n &{}\\cdot\\prod_{\\nu=1}^{J}\n \\left(\\Gamma(n_\\nu)\n       \\prod_{h:T_h=\\mathtt{00}}\\Gamma(a_{\\nu,h})\\right),\n\\end{split}$$ where $T_h$ is the tag at position $h$, and juxtaposition and products mean bitstring concatenation in increasing index order. The header, tag count, and group count delimit the entire list. A grouped packed stream is valid when this descriptor and its accompanying records have exactly the parameters, shapes, and orders just specified.",

For $V=MR$, its time is $$\\label{eq:packed-selected-time}\n O\\!\\left(V(L^\\tau+1)+MA^3+\n       M\\min\\{1,80(f-1)2^{-K}\\}\\,A(R+A)\\right).$$ When $A=O(p)$, $R$ exceeds every fixed polynomial in $p$, $f\\le d\\le p$, and $K/\\log p\\to\\infty$, this is $O(V(L^\\tau+1))$, uniformly over the group counts, shapes, and payloads whose supplied descriptors satisfy that bound.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-controlled-parity-toggle**: The displayed rotation schedule flips the parity of the target integer precisely when the Boolean control is set, while preserving its quotient under division by two.
- **invariant-arbitrary-auxiliary-restoration**: The completed rotation schedule restores the auxiliary integer for every initial auxiliary value.
- **invariant-zero-control-identity**: With an unset Boolean control, every operation in the schedule leaves both integers unchanged.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-certifying-asymptotic-multiplication-from-local-tests**: Treat successful finite rotation tests as certification of the multiplication running-time theorem. -> The tests check a local address identity and do not establish the network rank deficits, recursive tape costs, numerical precision bounds, or complete multiplication reduction.
- **anti-omitting-exceptional-address-repair**: Use the packed cyclic shifts alone as the complete selected-bit permutation. -> Carries and borrows can cross segment boundaries at exceptional addresses; agreement away from that set does not establish the required permutation everywhere.
- **anti-pure-routing-interpretation**: Transfer the address-interchange bound to a system that permits only movement of intact data. -> The construction computes intermediate XOR combinations, so its permitted operations exceed those of a movement-only system.
- **anti-premature-numerical-truncation**: Truncate intermediate coefficients after each gate of the accelerated butterfly network. -> Intermediate operators need not be contractions, so the completed-layer error argument does not justify inserting additional truncations within the network.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The paper supplies an explicit integer rotation schedule and its exact controlled parity-toggle identity. This panel implements that finite constructive ingredient and exhaustively checks the declared bounded domain, including negative integers and arbitrary auxiliary contents. It does not certify the packed exceptional-address repair, the fixed-tape running-time bounds, or the complete multiplication theorem.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with inclusive integer intervals u and w and a nonempty list c of Boolean controls.
# PRECONDITIONS: Interval endpoints are integers, ordered, bounded in magnitude by 100; controls are distinct integers 0 or 1.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of [control, initial_u, initial_w, final_u, final_w] rows in control, u, w order.
# TIME: Eight exact integer updates per enumerated input; serialization is proportional to the number of rows.
# SPACE: The output list stores one row per enumerated input.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact signed integers; modulo two uses the nonnegative remainder even for negative inputs.
# FAILURE: Invalid JSON or a violated input contract raises an exception and exits unsuccessfully.
import json
import sys

def interval(value):
    if not isinstance(value, list) or len(value) != 2:
        raise ValueError('invalid interval')
    lo, hi = value
    if type(lo) is not int or type(hi) is not int:
        raise ValueError('integer endpoints required')
    if not -100 <= lo <= hi <= 100:
        raise ValueError('interval outside checked contract')
    return range(lo, hi + 1)

def schedule(u, w, control):
    c = control
    u -= c * int(w % 2 == 0)
    w -= c * int(u % 2 == 0)
    u += c * int(w % 2 == 0)
    w += c * int(u % 2 == 0)
    w += c * int(u % 2 == 1)
    u += c * int(w % 2 == 1)
    w -= c * int(u % 2 == 1)
    u -= c * int(w % 2 == 1)
    return u, w

def main():
    data = json.load(sys.stdin)
    if not isinstance(data, dict) or set(data) != {'u', 'w', 'c'}:
        raise ValueError('invalid input fields')
    us = interval(data['u'])
    ws = interval(data['w'])
    controls = data['c']
    if not isinstance(controls, list) or not controls:
        raise ValueError('nonempty control list required')
    if any(type(c) is not int or c not in (0, 1) for c in controls):
        raise ValueError('Boolean controls required')
    if len(set(controls)) != len(controls):
        raise ValueError('duplicate control')
    output = []
    for c in controls:
        for u in us:
            for w in ws:
                final_u, final_w = schedule(u, w, c)
                output.append([c, u, w, final_u, final_w])
    json.dump(output, sys.stdout, separators=(',', ':'))
    sys.stdout.write('\n')

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs exact multiplication, division with remainder, and integer square root on fixed finite-tape machines. These are implementable specifications for a tape-machine simulator, although the stated constants and thresholds limit their practical value. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The numerical construction evaluates polynomial coefficients and selected-bit butterfly layers. It supplies no game-state update, collision calculation, or physical simulation contract. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The transposition reduction gives a concrete contract for a sequential matrix-processing tool: convert the shape to a total bit length, invoke the multiplication-based transposition procedure, and serialize the transposed entries. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The arbitrary-width interchange procedure realizes an exact address permutation on a complete Cartesian array while preserving the gap, prefix, suffix, and payload. Its construction includes padding, recursive field interchanges, unpadding, and cleanup. |
| data_structures | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The grouped packed stream is a concrete sequential representation for rectangles with different spectator lengths. Its supplied descriptor identifies the common field template and each group's shape, allowing selected-bit addition while preserving group boundaries and payloads. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite networks are constructive algebraic ingredients, but their tape implementation establishes no accelerator architecture, hardware throughput, or memory-hierarchy performance contract. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-tape-cost-model** (formal_scope): The multiplication bound concerns deterministic computation with a fixed finite alphabet and a fixed finite number of one-dimensional tapes. It supplies no hardware throughput or unit-cost random-access performance guarantee. Blocked use: ['Claiming the same improvement for arbitrary random-access architectures without a separate cost analysis.', 'Ignoring tape-head movement when accounting for execution time.']
- **limit-asymptotic-practical-performance** (formal_scope): The construction establishes an asymptotic bit-complexity bound with extremely large constants and thresholds. Practical arithmetic-library speedups are not established. Blocked use: ['Advertising an observed practical speedup from the asymptotic exponent alone.', 'Treating the construction as a benchmarked production arithmetic implementation.']
- **limit-promised-binary-interface** (hypothesis): Exact multiplication assumes equally wide binary operands and returns the prescribed padded binary product. Leading zeroes belong to the supplied input width. Blocked use: ['Applying the stated input contract directly to unequal-width operands without padding.', 'Removing required leading output zeroes.']
- **limit-ordered-affine-controls** (hypothesis): Linear-volume controlled shifts require the target to follow its controls, fixed valid rational coefficients, and the stated offset-preparation bound. Unit scalings additionally require invertible numerators. Blocked use: ['Applying the linear-volume bound to a target preceding its controls.', 'Using a nonunit coefficient as an invertible scaling.', 'Allowing uncharged growing coefficient descriptions or offset computation.']
- **limit-finite-network-frame-contract** (hypothesis): The recursive saving requires fixed circuit data, common gate frames, the endpoint shear identity on every role, and a strict edge-rank deficit. Scratch roles participate in the endpoint identity. Blocked use: ['Replacing the finite network with an arbitrary linear circuit while retaining its cost saving.', 'Checking endpoint behavior only on data-bank roles.', 'Assigning different frames to incidences of the same gate.']
- **limit-computation-versus-routing** (formal_scope): Address interchange forms intermediate XOR combinations. Its final permutation does not place it inside a model restricted to moving intact data. Blocked use: ['Transferring the bound to routing-only systems.', 'Claiming that intermediate encodings always remain unchanged payload records.']
- **limit-power-width-row-divisibility** (hypothesis): The power-width interchange interface requires the stated power widths, a suitable fixed prime, and a preceding row field with the required divisibility. General widths need the separate padding and width-reduction construction. Blocked use: ['Invoking the power-width recurrence on arbitrary unprepared row counts.', 'Omitting the high-digit movement, padding, or unpadding needed for general widths.']
- **limit-complete-cartesian-address-ranges** (hypothesis): The chunk and packed-address procedures operate on complete Cartesian ranges in lexicographic order. Sparse address lists and coordinate-dependent suffix lengths do not satisfy these interfaces directly. Blocked use: ['Applying the same permutation cost to sparse or irregular address collections without a reduction.', 'Using a row whose suffix shape depends on coordinates within that row.']
- **limit-packed-exceptional-repair** (hypothesis): Packed rotations agree with the intended selected-bit permutation outside the identified exceptional set. Exact exceptional-record correction is necessary for correctness on all addresses and payloads. Blocked use: ['Using the rotations alone as the complete packed permutation.', 'Assuming guard failures are harmless because their fraction is small.', 'Replacing deterministic repair with a probabilistic omission.']
- **limit-packed-asymptotic-simplification** (hypothesis): The simplified packed-operation cost requires the stated descriptor bound, superpolynomial record growth, selected-axis bound, and increasing bit spacing. Outside that family, the explicit descriptor and exceptional-sort terms remain part of the cost. Blocked use: ['Discarding descriptor arithmetic when records are short.', 'Dropping exceptional-sort costs for fixed or insufficient selected-bit spacing.']
- **limit-admissible-layer-parameters** (hypothesis): The accelerated simultaneous layer requires admissible fixed rational exponents and comparison constants, sufficient precision beyond its cutoff, valid headers, complete address chunks, and the stated component widths. Blocked use: ['Claiming the accelerated layer bound for arbitrary dimensions and polynomial lengths.', 'Ignoring descriptor validity or record-width limits.', 'Treating its asymptotic cutoff as absent.']
- **limit-exact-intermediate-layer-arithmetic** (approximation): The accelerated layer evaluates intermediate Gaussian dyadics exactly with reserved denominator and magnitude guards. Its error guarantee concerns the final truncation after the completed layer. Blocked use: ['Truncating after each internal gate under the same error bound.', 'Replacing guarded exact arithmetic with ordinary floating-point arithmetic without a separate error proof.']
- **limit-synthetic-roots-and-retained-order** (hypothesis): Synthetic character cancellation uses the stated power-of-two roots in the negacyclic quotient ring. Forward transforms retain a positional frequency layout that the opposite procedure must consume consistently. Blocked use: ['Dividing by a potentially noninvertible character difference in the quotient ring.', 'Mixing different retained frequency orders during pointwise multiplication.', 'Treating the opposite normalized transform as an unscaled inverse.']
- **limit-signed-packing-and-normalisation** (hypothesis): Signed packing requires disk-grid polynomial inputs, the specified power-of-two polynomial length and component format, centered coefficient recovery, and negacyclic wrapping. The returned truncated polynomial is normalized rather than the unscaled exact product. Blocked use: ['Decoding negative coefficients as unsigned packing digits.', 'Replacing negacyclic subtraction with cyclic addition.', 'Returning the normalized polynomial as the unscaled product.', 'Using coefficient magnitudes outside the proved separation bound.']
- **limit-convolution-output-disk-margin** (approximation): The synthetic convolution approximation has an amplified error and a known enlarged output component width. A disk-grid output requires the additional smaller-operand norm and error-margin condition. Blocked use: ['Assuming the scaled output always lies in the unit disk for arbitrary disk operands.', 'Discarding sign-extension bits before establishing that the narrowed width can represent the output.', 'Omitting the exact final convolution scale.']
- **limit-resampling-separation-and-error-units** (hypothesis): Ordered Gaussian resampling requires coprime separated source and target lengths and the stated precision and Gaussian-parameter bounds. Its reported error uses a precision-scaled norm, while tensor composition and axis movement retain their separate charges. Blocked use: ['Applying the identity to arbitrary noncoprime lengths.', 'Interpreting the scaled error bound as an unscaled absolute error.', 'Ignoring accumulated linewise errors or axis exposure costs.']
- **limit-chirp-periodicity-and-matched-permutations** (hypothesis): The chirp construction requires even target lengths. Source-permutation cancellation depends on matched forward permutations, pointwise multiplication, the opposite Fourier sign, and the stated normalizations. Blocked use: ['Using the same chirp periodicity argument for odd target lengths.', 'Combining unmatched source frequency permutations.', 'Removing either convolution normalization without adjusting exact recovery.']
- **limit-prime-axis-and-coefficient-capacity** (hypothesis): The multiplication assembly deterministically constructs distinct prime source axes and proves sufficient coefficient capacity to avoid nonzero cyclic wraparound. Arbitrary axis choices do not automatically satisfy separation or capacity. Blocked use: ['Treating prime lengths as uncharged advice.', 'Choosing a source box too small for the ordinary coefficient convolution.', 'Replacing the generated source lengths without checking the resampling hypotheses.']
- **limit-strict-rounding-and-carry-order** (approximation): Exact recovery requires reversing both convolution normalizations and digit scales, proving a strict nearest-integer error margin, restoring ordinary coefficient order, and then propagating radix carries. Blocked use: ['Rounding before the final exact rescalings.', 'Accepting a nonstrict rounding-error boundary.', 'Propagating carries in tensor address order instead of ordinary coefficient order.', 'Dropping required output padding.']
- **limit-positive-divisor-and-exact-final-tests** (hypothesis): Division requires a positive divisor. Newton approximations are followed by exact remainder or square tests to recover the required integer answers, including exact quotients and perfect squares. Blocked use: ['Invoking the division contract with a zero divisor.', 'Returning a rounded Newton approximation without the exact correction tests.']
- **limit-grouped-descriptor-and-locality** (hypothesis): Grouped selected-bit addition requires a valid descriptor matching its record stream, complete per-group rectangles, and a bound on the actual total descriptor length. Exceptional records are corrected within their own group. Blocked use: ['Bounding only the header while omitting group entries or record-width information.', 'Allowing exceptional records to migrate between groups.', 'Using inconsistent descriptor shapes and record counts.']
- **limit-companion-fourier-model** (formal_scope): The companion exact Fourier-transform application uses a separate exact complex-register model. Sharing its finite network does not establish a stable floating-point transform or identify its cost with fixed-tape bit complexity. Blocked use: ['Transferring the companion exponent directly to integer bit complexity.', 'Claiming floating-point stability from the exact-register construction.']
- **limit-transposition-reduction-scope** (formal_scope): The transposition consequence concerns positive matrix dimensions and entry widths, row-major serialization, and the cited fixed-tape reduction. It establishes no matrix multiplication algorithm or practical external-storage speedup. Blocked use: ['Reclassifying the transposition result as matrix multiplication.', 'Omitting descriptor computation and multiplication-interface conversions from the reduction cost.']
- **limit-local-finite-verification** (formal_scope): Finite checks of the scalar rotation identity can verify only their declared input domain. They do not certify the universal identity, exceptional-address repair, recursive tape implementation, numerical bounds, or the complete multiplication theorem. Blocked use: ['Promoting local finite-domain agreement to certification of the asymptotic theorem.', 'Using successful scalar tests as evidence that packed exceptional-address repair is implemented correctly.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give a deterministic algorithm that multiplies two <i>n</i>-bit integers in $`O(n(\lg n)^{1-\kappa})`$ worst-case time, with $`\kappa=2^{-182}`$, on one fixed finite-alphabet Turing machine with a fixed finite number of one-dimensional tapes. The algorithm is exact for every input length and disproves the $`n\log n`$ optimality conjecture of Schönhage and Strassen in this model.

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

Schönhage and Strassen proved in 1971 that two $n$-bit integers can be multiplied in $O(n\log n\log\log n)$ time on a multitape Turing machine, and proposed $n\log n$ as the optimal order of growth (Schönhage and Strassen 1971, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[SchonhageStrassen1971](#ref-SchonhageStrassen1971)]. After a sequence of improvements, Harvey and van der Hoeven reached the unconditional $O(n\log n)$ upper bound (Harvey and Hoeven 2021) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[HarveyHoeven2021](#ref-HarveyHoeven2021)]. We obtain a strict power saving in the logarithmic factor, in the same fixed finite-tape model. The algorithm uses linear combinations of intermediate data to reduce the cost of both address rearrangement and Fourier-transform layers.

For a binary string $x$ of length $n$, let $\operatorname{val}(x)$ be its nonnegative integer value, with the leftmost bit most significant. For $0\le z<2^k$, let $\operatorname{bin}_k(z)$ denote its binary representation padded on the left to length $k$. We consider exact multiplication: on input $x\#y$, with $x,y\in\{0,1\}^n$, the output must be $\operatorname{bin}_{2n}(\operatorname{val}(x)\operatorname{val}(y))$. Write $\lg n=\max\{\lceil\log_2 n\rceil,1\}$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There is one deterministic Turing machine $A$, with a fixed finite alphabet and a fixed finite number of one-dimensional tapes, that computes this exact product for every $n\ge1$ and every pair of $n$-bit inputs. Its worst-case running time satisfies $$T_A(n)=O\!\left(n(\lg n)^{1-\kappa}\right),
 \qquad \kappa=2^{-182}.$$*

</div>

The bound is asymptotic through all input lengths. The constants and thresholds in the construction are extremely large; the algorithm is intended to establish a bit-complexity bound. The theorem refutes an eventual $\Omega(n\log n)$ lower bound for every fixed multiplication machine in this model.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The two tape operations

Splitting the inputs into radix digits reduces multiplication to convolution of short integer coefficients. Our starting point is the Gaussian-resampling reduction of Harvey and van der Hoeven (Harvey and Hoeven 2021) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[HarveyHoeven2021](#ref-HarveyHoeven2021)]: it replaces the Fourier transforms needed for this convolution by multidimensional transforms whose axis lengths are powers of two. After a further conversion to convolution, one coordinate is stored as the coefficient list of a polynomial modulo $y^r+1$, with $r$ a power of two. Powers of $y$ then serve as roots of unity in the remaining axes, and multiplication by such a root is a signed shift of the coefficient list. These are the synthetic polynomial transforms of Nussbaumer and Quandalle and Nussbaumer (Nussbaumer and Quandalle 1978; Nussbaumer 1980) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[NussbaumerQuandalle1978](#ref-NussbaumerQuandalle1978), [Nussbaumer1980](#ref-Nussbaumer1980)].

In this representation, the transform calculation consists of address rearrangements, signed shifts, and two-point butterfly operations. Scanning an array of $\Theta(n)$ stored bits at each of $\Theta(\log n)$ transform levels already costs $\Theta(n\log n)$. We therefore need savings in both movement and arithmetic. Two tape procedures supply them; in both bounds, $V$ denotes the stored bit volume.

The first procedure interchanges two disjoint contiguous address fields of $u$ bits in an array whose addresses form a complete Cartesian product. Its cost is $O(Vu^\tau)$ for a fixed $\tau<1$. Although its final effect is an exact permutation, its intermediate steps form pointwise XOR combinations of data. The final permutation is exact for arbitrary initial auxiliary values.

The second procedure applies $$H_0(u,v)=\bigl((u+v)/2,(u-v)/2\bigr)$$ simultaneously on selected coordinate bits of the numerical arrays. Each record is a polynomial with complex fixed-point coefficients, and $H_0$ acts coefficientwise. On the layouts used in the multiplication algorithm, one selects the same bit position in each of at most $d$ equal-width address chunks, and the cost is $O(Vd^{\lambda'})$ for a fixed $\lambda'<1$. Intermediate values are exact Gaussian dyadics, whose real and imaginary parts have power-of-two denominators. A single truncation follows the completed contractive layer. Section <a href="#sec:tape-interchange" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tape-interchange">4</a> proves the address bound, and Section <a href="#sec:fast-butterfly-layers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fast-butterfly-layers">5</a> gives the numerical layout, precision hypotheses, and layer bound.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Linear networks and the recursive saving

Each procedure arises from its own fixed linear network that exchanges two banks of values, with prescribed signs, and restores every auxiliary value. A wire’s fixed place in the network is called its role. To use the network on an array, regard a row as the complete ordered suffix, including all active address fields, at a fixed prefix and row index. Distribute rows cyclically among the $W$ roles, padding the row count for each prefix to a multiple of $W$. Let $V$ now denote the node volume after padding. All role streams have the same local address set and each contains $V/W$ bits. A scalar gate is then applied pointwise to matching addresses on the roles that it touches.

The representation of a role’s array may change along the network. At a source, gate, or sink $v$, choose an invertible linear operator $D_v$ on the space of arrays indexed by the common address set. We call $D_v$ its frame: the stored array is $D_vg$ when the logical array is $g$. All incidences of one gate have the same frame. Along an edge from $u$ to $v$, the operator $D_vD_u^{-1}$ changes the stored array from $D_ug$ to $D_vg$. At a pointwise linear gate $G$, the common frame commutes with the gate: $$G(D_vg_1,\ldots,D_vg_t)=D_vG(g_1,\ldots,g_t).$$ The supplied input needs no preliminary change of frame: its logical interpretation is defined by applying the inverse input frame. Thus the intermediate frames cancel, and a route from input role $w$ to output role $\rho(w)$ applies $D_{\mathrm{sink}(\rho(w))}D_{\mathrm{source}(w)}^{-1}$, together with its prescribed scalar sign. Undoing the role exchange and those signs leaves the endpoint operator on every role array, including the roles that carried auxiliary scalar values.

For the bit procedure, that endpoint operator is an address shear: two fields, written as vectors $H,D$ over a common residue ring, are updated by $(H,D)\mapsto(H+D,D)$. Two further linear-cost address updates complete their interchange. For the numerical procedure, the endpoint operator becomes the simultaneous $H_0$ layer after diagonal phase operations and a dyadic scale. The intermediate frames are chosen so that every edge can be implemented by smaller calls to the same procedure.

The finite construction in Section <a href="#sec:finite-networks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite-networks">3</a> attaches subspaces of a fixed $m$-dimensional space to its gates and terminals. The subspaces at adjacent vertices are nested. Their dimension changes count the smaller operations needed on an edge, with an additional source contribution in the bit construction. The construction uses three-element subsets: gather and scatter create coefficients depending on intersection size, and auxiliary wires cancel the unwanted coefficients. Orthogonality of the corresponding indicator vectors permits the subspace assignments. The decisive property is that the total number $s$ of smaller calls satisfies $s<Wm$.

A recursive call at parameter $mf$ therefore uses $s$ calls at parameter $f$, each on a stream of volume $V/W$. The parameter counts field digits for address interchange and selected axes for a numerical layer. After dividing cost by volume, multiplying the parameter by $m$ increases the recursive contribution by $s/W<m$. This strict inequality is the source of the power saving. The later tape and arithmetic estimates preserve it, including the work of changing numerical address coordinates. Those changes are implemented by whole-chunk moves and controlled shifts, followed by a deterministic correction of the explicitly identified exceptional addresses.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### From transforms to the exact product

The remaining proof places these procedures inside the multiplication reduction and accounts for every change of representation. First, the radix-digit coefficient list is placed on a product of distinct prime cyclic axes by a Chinese-remainder map that preserves convolution. Section <a href="#sec:assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:assembly">9</a> implements this map through triangular controlled shifts and charged axis moves. Gaussian resampling then expresses each source Fourier transform through a power-of-two tensor transform. Section <a href="#sec:resampling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:resampling">8</a> retains the source and target coordinate permutations in the transform identity, so they need not be executed as separate full sorts.

Next, Bluestein’s chirp identity converts the middle transform, including its target permutation, into normalized cyclic convolution. A phase twist changes the last cyclic coordinate into a polynomial modulo $y^r+1$; its negative wrap gives precisely the original cyclic wrap after the twist is removed. Sections <a href="#sec:synthetic-layouts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:synthetic-layouts">6</a> and <a href="#sec:exact-ring-products" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exact-ring-products">7</a> compute convolution on the remaining axes over this polynomial ring. The two forward synthetic transforms retain the same frequency order through their pointwise product, and the opposite transform consumes that order. Their polynomial products use signed packing and the established $O(n\log n)$ multiplier as an ordinary subroutine.

Finally, the source permutation retained during resampling cancels across the two forward source transforms, their pointwise product, and the opposite-sign transform. Section <a href="#sec:assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:assembly">9</a> removes the known normalizations and digit scales, proves that the recovered coefficient error is strictly below $1/2$, and propagates carries to obtain the exact binary product. A compatible choice of rational parameters makes all terms in its cost table fit Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Figure <a href="#fig:multiplication-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:multiplication-flow">1</a> summarizes these reductions.

<figure id="fig:multiplication-flow">

<figcaption>The reductions connecting the tape procedures to multiplication. The arrows describe changes of problem and representation. Fast address interchanges organize the layouts, and simultaneous butterfly layers accelerate the synthetic transforms. Solving the final polynomial-ring convolution evaluates the transforms needed in the preceding stages. Known rescalings, rounding, and carry propagation then recover the exact integer product.</figcaption>
</figure>
